# OpenZCAD master roadmap

**Single source of truth for priorities, dependencies and delivery status.**
Consolidated 2026-09-12 against OpenZCAD `main` at `28d1551f` (PR #315) and
reconciled 2026-09-17 against `main` at `3d7fdc33` (#316–#356).
Facts refreshed 2026-11-11 against `main` at `ff8fcbca` (#1344): canonical
documents use schema v54 (`PROJECT_DOCUMENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` in
[packages/shared/src/index.ts](packages/shared/src/index.ts); per-version
history lives in that file and the evidence of the row that added it), and
the paired `remus-wasm` and `remus-wasm-io` packages are version
`2026.1.132`, pinned to immutable Remus commit
`a014f15cffb7ffde0f3b8eae0a911528a6f55f91` in
[the adapter manifest](packages/kernel-adapter/package.json) and
[pnpm-lock.yaml](pnpm-lock.yaml). This source snapshot is not a fresh
qualification of upstream Remus `main` or a production-site acceptance report.

Revised 2026-09-26 against a feature inventory of a mainstream direct-modeling
CAD ([comparison record](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md)); that pass added
U05–U07, S07, S08, F05, M10–M13, I04, I05, L08 and L09 and named slices inside
existing rows. It changed no delivery status.

Expanded 2026-09-29 with a file-interoperability scope: I06–I20 add format
qualification and native-CAD translation; D01/D04, I02 and I04 retain their
existing PDF, DXF, STEP-fidelity and export-option ownership. The
[format specification](docs/plans/file-format-interoperability.md) defines
directions, priorities and acceptance; these are planned capabilities.

Reviewed 2026-09-30 through the installed FreeCAD 1.1.3 interface
([hands-on comparison](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md)).
This pass sharpens existing sketch, expression, reference, drawing and assembly
acceptance; it adds no delivery claim or new specialist-workbench lane.

Reviewed 2026-10-14 in a full project review
([record](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)): 39 confirmed bugs and 16
improvements across persistence, sharing, import, the kernel boundary, the
viewport and tooling. Its fix PRs record their evidence in the rows they touch;
the remaining findings are assigned to the master rows below; this evidence update changes no delivery status.

Start here to choose work. The documents in [Supporting plans](#supporting-plans)
retain specifications, acceptance details and historical evidence; their old
phase numbers, priorities and status tables no longer define independent queues.
ADRs retain architecture decisions. Record delivery status here through
[evidence fragments](docs/roadmap-evidence/README.md), and update a
supporting specification only when its contract changes.

## Analytics integration

| Master ID | Status | Scope and evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A01 | Partial | Glimpse: backend counters; fresh opt-in diagnostic/feature/size/part aggregates and user-shared reports; separate expiring cloud-account/storage census with no exported identifiers. [Setup and privacy boundaries](docs/glimpse.md). Regression coverage in `analytics.test.ts` and `worker/glimpse.test.ts`; production collector and consent migrations verified; activation enables both deployment switches while preserving fresh browser and separate account consent. Live delivery is checked during rollout. Local-only reopen probes now skip absent account copies while genuine failures remain observable (`accountProjectLoad.test.ts`, `local-project-cloud-load.spec.ts`); live rollout remains separate. 2026-11-05 WS3 refusal harvest: opt-in aggregate `kernel_refused` event whose bucket carries only the refusing operation, the kernel refusal family/operation/category/code, the warning kind and the target/neighbour surface types, each from a fixed vocabulary validated by the mirrored `glimpseContract.ts` in client and relay ([Kernel refusal harvest](docs/glimpse.md#kernel-refusal-harvest-ws3)). `analytics.test.ts` plants names, ids, hashes, dimensions and file names in every field and proves none reach the wire; `worker/glimpse.test.ts` proves the relay rejects non-canonical buckets and extra keys. The Glimpse service still has to mirror the vocabulary before it records the event. PR #1255 review fixes retain the real `direct-edit` family and operation in the client/relay vocabulary. Only user edit checks enter the harvester; background copies, demos, baseline/lineage builds and assistant analysis cannot count refusals or interfere with captures. `useRefusalHarvest.test.tsx` covers project/version changes and out-of-order replies. |

## Where we are

OpenZCAD has a working browser CAD foundation: exact solid modeling, editable
feature history, expressions, direct manipulation, local persistence, optional
cloud sync and collaboration. The largest remaining product gaps are complete
sketch constraint workflows, reference geometry, drawings and assemblies.
Recent work improved existing modeling and the bounded imported-holder workflow,
and the 14 September kernel-adoption wave (#330–#342) moved booleans, patterns,
blends, mesh import, sketch modify tools and sections onto typed kernel entry
points; it did not complete those larger milestones.
The 26 September comparison with a mainstream direct-modeling CAD confirmed
those four gaps and added a fifth that needs no kernel work: the workspace
conventions every mainstream tool shares (a full hotkey set, saved views, item
groups, history step actions, notable-point measurement picks, X-ray display). It also
recorded that the reference CAD ships drawings, visualization and share links
with no assemblies at all, which supports the milestone order below.
The FreeCAD walkthrough reinforces the same sequence: actionable sketch feedback,
coherent expression/feature editing and a dimensioned drawing handoff, then
component instances and grounding before joint solving. Keep the quiet-stage
workspace; breadth of a reference CAD is not a reason to copy its toolbar density.

| Delivered baseline | Evidence and boundary |
| --- | --- |

| Editable hole, mirror, split, shell, offset, draft, thicken, loft/sweep/helical-sweep history; fillet retargeting | #307, #309, #311, #314; `test/e2e/profile-feature-edit.spec.ts`. Advanced feature options remain M01–M06. |

Restore/branch browser coverage and capture-on-leave are already implemented; live rollout evidence remains L01. |

Remaining string seams are the recorded allowances in W04. |

Probe doubles boolean rebuild cost (measured 2.03×); K05 owns the remainder. |

**Status vocabulary:** `Open` means a defined remainder; `Partial` means a
bounded subset is merged; `Complete` means the row's contract is merged and
only live acceptance, if any, remains; `Revalidate` means the older finding
lacks current proof and must be reproduced before implementation; `Deferred`
means deliberately outside the next delivery sequence. No other status is
used: a row whose work has merged is `Partial` or `Complete`, never "In
review", "Planned" or "Done". No feature row below is claimed actively
owned without a linked open implementation PR. “Delivered” means merged source
with referenced evidence; production acceptance is recorded separately.

## Delivery order

Finish useful workflows before adding a second way to do the same action.
Re-baselined 2026-11-11 with the owner to match where the work has actually
gone since 21 October: the workspace and tool-interaction overhaul, end-to-end
encryption, assemblies, iPad/touch and printable mechanical features are the
active lanes and come first. Milestones 0–3 stay the queue behind them and the
place to work when an active lane is blocked on an owner or outside input.
Kernel/reliability work (K-, W- and O-rows, with [K10](#k10) the current
campaign) runs alongside only where dependencies and touched files are
disjoint. Each row is an outcome, not necessarily one PR. Split a large row
into named, bounded slices under the same ID before coding; never treat a
partial slice as the whole row's completion.

| Order | Lane or milestone | Exit signal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Active | Workspace and tool interaction ([U08](#u08)–[U11](#u11)) | U08 and U09 are complete. U11 carries the helical-sweep interaction (ordered picks, at most two handles on the result, rare choices behind Options) to thread, gear, booleans and patterns, one PR per tool with an e2e driven only by picks and drags. U10's lookdev slices are judged against its fixed captures in the production renderer. |
| Active | End-to-end encryption ([L11](#l11)) | Written outside-review clearance with no open P0/P1, then default-on, the plaintext freeze and the deletion PR, each under [ADR-024](docs/adrs/ADR-024-end-to-end-encrypted-projects.md) and its own authorization. Until then, remaining acceptance and migration/legacy-reader qualification. |
| Active | Assemblies ([AS01](#as01)–[AS09](#as09)) | AS01/AS02/AS06/AS07 meet their contracts (offline save/reopen, saved transforms and conflict behavior), then AS03 BOM and exploded views; AS04/AS05/AS08/AS09 follow only when their dependencies qualify. |
| Active | iPad, touch and Apple Pencil ([L02](#l02)) | All eight slices have merged source; each closes only with real-iPad evidence for its device-only behavior and measured rendering budgets. Mouse and trackpad input stays unchanged. |
| Active | Printable mechanical features ([M14](#m14), [M15](#m15), [D06](#d06)) | M14 the meshing-pair helper and partial-span teeth (bevel, worm and enveloping gears have merged); M15 tapered threads and chamfers on a thread that stops short of its face (cosmetic threads and callouts stay with M01); D06 auto-dimensioning through its design slices. |
| 0 | Close the shipped workflow gaps | H01 has an explicit acceptance matrix; H02 has measured cold/warm behavior; U01/U02 resolve reproduced command/value/feedback disagreements; L01 records outstanding live checks. |
| 1 | Complete the modeling foundation | S01–S08, R01, F01–F05 and U05–U07 meet their row contracts: durable dimensions, understandable constraints, editable sketch geometry, useful reference/parameter surfaces, and the keyboard, history/items and viewing conventions of a mainstream tool. |
| 2 | Deepen part modeling and inspection | Selected M01–M13, I01, I04–I08 slices work through creation, revision and export with their kernel prerequisites proved. |
| 3 | Drawings MVP | D01 then a bounded D02 slice: aligned scaled views plus a useful dimension, explicit revision update/invalidation, offline reopen and PDF handoff. D03/D04 add sections and DXF; the milestone is a dimensioned shop handoff, not outlines alone. |
| Later | Reach, automation and new body types | Select L03–L09, L12, D05, K09, M12 or I09–I20 from a measured use case; do not expand every lane at once. |

Assemblies were milestone 4; they moved to the active lanes when AS01, AS02,
AS06 and AS07 merged (#1182). L02 moved from Later when it was selected on
2026-11-05.

**Next bounded picks (reviewed 2026-11-11; no implementation PR was open):**

1. [U11](#u11): the next tool after helical sweep, thread ([M15](#m15)), in
   the shared pick-and-drag interaction; then gear, booleans and patterns.
2. [AS03](#as03): deterministic BOM and stored exploded-view offsets, after
   recording the AS01/AS02 offline save/reopen and conflict cases that remain.
3. [M14](#m14) the meshing-pair helper that places a joint at the right center
   distance (joins the M14 and AS02 work), then partial-span teeth.
4. [L11](#l11) remaining acceptance and migration/legacy-reader qualification.
   Outside-review clearance, default-on and deletion wait on the owner and
   the reviewer, not on an agent.
5. [L02](#l02) and [U10](#u10): only device or capture evidence remains;
   these need an iPad or the lookdev captures, so record that evidence when
   it is available rather than adding slices.

When every active pick is blocked on owner or outside input, take milestone 0–1
remainders: [H02](#h02) quiet-host and target-device timing and whole-worker
retention, [H01](#h01) live acceptance from its matrix, [S02](#s02) and
[S04](#s04) remaining targets, [S08](#s08) inference while drawing, and
[F01](#f01)/[F03](#f03). Refresh `main`, the kernel pin and open PRs before
choosing; live acceptance needing credentials or source data stays pending
without blocking independent work. This list replaces the 2026-10-21 picks and
the conflicting next lists in older plans.

These milestones are not a global gate: independent foundation work can run
while live acceptance is pending, and drawing design can start before every
part-modeling row is complete. No calendar estimates are carried forward from
audits of earlier interfaces.

## 0. Close the shipped workflow gaps

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / supporting detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="h01"></a>H01 | Partial | Parameter edits now preflight dependent geometry, reject measured-limit violations and preserve the last valid model; Tweak shows limits and refusals. Local regression coverage: `parameter-validation.spec.ts`, `growing-holder.spec.ts`. Close holder acceptance: audit existing browser/Vitest coverage, add missing equivalent-unit and worker-interruption cases, specify/test ambiguous drawing handling, then record the original-source import → proposal → combined edits → undo/reopen → STEP/STL flow on the deployed revision. Record synthetic, local-private and live evidence separately. Delivered 2026-09-17: the [acceptance matrix](docs/qa/2026-09-17/holder-acceptance-matrix.md) names every Phase 7 case with its evidence and tier; recognition margins now scale with document units (`test/growing-holder-units.test.ts`), the recipe build in an inch document is a pinned kernel refusal at the bridge union (K07), ambiguity is pinned end to end (`test/growing-holder-ambiguous.test.ts`), and reloads mid-Apply and mid-edit leave the document whole (`test/e2e/growing-holder-interruption.spec.ts`). Remaining: the live walkthrough on the deployed revision with the private source (checklist in the matrix), and the guided pick after ambiguity (H03). Since consolidation: assistant text toggles (#319), moved/rotated holders parameterize in place with lettering riding rigidly (#338, #351; `test/growing-holder-lettering.test.ts`, `test/e2e/growing-holder.spec.ts`). Rotated input is still refused unless the opening stays world-axis aligned. 2026-10-02 candidate: assistant follow-ups retain the originating selection intent, and explicit selected-edge subsets stay scoped during grounding; ambiguous multi-body selections refuse. Contract and panel regressions cover preview/apply targeting. 2026-10-14 review fix (this PR): on-demand imported-face recognition no longer scales document units a second time, so an inch document reads a 5 mm bore as 0.197 in, matching the measure pass (`test/imported-kernel-recognized-features.test.ts`). Full-suite follow-up qualifies the inch recipe through 2-inch growth and millimetre STEP replay, with strict solids, closed display meshes, measured bounds and independent material witnesses. Mesh closure searches adjacent quantization cells within the existing weld tolerance instead of rejecting coincident f32 boundaries at a grid split. Linear recognition rounds at the same physical precision in every unit, avoiding inch-dependent recipe shifts. Countersunk bore shrinking retains broken-out cone trims and certifies split circular rim coverage; native and STEP tests check closed-form added volume, unchanged retained carriers and source geometry, and watertight STEP replay. Four supported wedge end-cap fillets now have independent inner/outer closed-form volume oracles. The synthetic STEP fixture is regenerated from the pinned exporter, thread refusals use typed codes, and portable synthetic recovery tests preserve production tmpfs/swap and resolved-path guards. The six formerly failing archive suites also join the CI selection; no assertion or gate is removed. Large mesh sewing now stays within the pinned native arena's tolerance ceiling, so inch-sized imports remain readable through cached replay; millimetre, inch and enlarged fixtures verify strict solids, bounds and volume after serialization into a fresh kernel. Browser regressions cover invitation dismissal during document loading, last-valid exact drag previews, fillet rearming, font retry recovery and repeated unitless mesh imports. Library action menus stay inside the visible shelf body rather than hiding their first action behind its toolbar; desktop and phone browser coverage checks menu containment, opening properties and unchanged saved documents. Validated commits register timing intent before publishing their geometry, retaining installation and frame evidence when a preflight result is reused. The complete browser sweep also exposed reentrant assembly animation scheduling: synchronous pose notifications started a second frame loop. Playback now permits one pending frame across joint sweeps, studies and tool previews; regression coverage checks sustained motion, pause, completion and unmount without modifying the saved document. Drag continuity checks measure the rendered face boundary for both valid preview routes. A holed-body regression forces the exact worker stream and holds final validation while pressing the retained handle, preserving the selection, validation-lock and no-flash assertions. Successive drags also run against a manually held analysis window; the test requires all four edits and their cumulative 5 mm rendered boundary. Section controls retain their viewport clamp while opening and while compact rails reflow; light and dark browser checks require the complete DXF action to remain directly clickable at four viewport sizes, including short landscape. Running commands consume context clicks on empty space as well as geometry, including where a rim stood before its fillet preview moved it. Multi-edge fillet coverage preserves both input picks across all three cases. Project-shelf navigation follows the stable Back to projects action while the responsive header hides its optional Beta badge; save-failure and local reopening assertions retain their original storage and request checks. Legacy demo fixtures release their live workspace save owner before rewriting IndexedDB. They prove the old record exists and await the parameter, repaired placement and repair revision together in one committed snapshot. Sweep path clicks reframe after the staged sketch arrives and after each exact preview, checking projected points stay on the canvas and each path segment becomes valid before continuing. Panel drag picks wait for camera rest and the current hover frame instead of accepting a stale cursor. Section placement excludes CSS transitions of its translation, including under reduced-motion's global duration override. Browser coverage requires programmatic placement to move the visible panel within the same frame and keeps controls reachable through the Computing → Exact size change. A failed feature now passes its input body through like a manual suppression (ADR-017 November 2026 amendment): only the failed step reports failure, its dependents build on its unchanged input and are flagged "after failed step" in History, reference repairs are withheld downstream, and commit gates still refuse the breaking edit. Synthetic coverage: `test/failed-feature-pass-through.test.ts`, `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-suppression.test.ts`. Local-private evidence: replaying the reported fillet-radius history at r 3 mm, failures fell from 20 to 14 and "target is unavailable" from 9 to 0. Hash-only picks no longer stay fragile once their entity is named: a clean committed rebuild now writes the body's v5 reference beside the stored hash of every hole, thicken, thread, on-body gear, shell-opening, draft and face-distance-opposite face pick, as it already did for fillet/chamfer edges and a direct edit's face, all or nothing and never past the first failed, refused or suppressed step; an upstream resize then resolves the pick by lineage (ADR-013 H01 hash-only upgrade amendment; `test/hash-only-reference-upgrade.test.ts`, `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-reference-repair.test.ts`, `topologyReferenceRepairs.test.ts`). A commit that stores a new unnamed pick says so once, and Diagnostics counts the steps that hold one (`fragileSelections.test.ts`, `DiagnosticsPanel.test.tsx`). Forked histories (two active features replacing the same body, so two overlapping solids of one part are built) are now reported on the later branch as an attributed `history-fork` warning that no commit gate reads, and `CommandManager` refuses an edit that creates or retargets a feature onto a replaced body, except at the rollback marker; stored logs replay unchanged and Duplicate no longer offers Pattern (ADR-017 November 2026 "forked histories" amendment; stable body lineage identity proposed in docs/adrs/ADR-025-stable-body-lineage-identity.md). Synthetic coverage: `packages/document-core/src/body-consumption.test.ts`, `test/history-fork.test.ts`, `test/duplicate-step.test.ts`. Resuming a rolled-back History (Resume full history, or moving the marker later) rebuilds the candidate first and, when resumed features would fail, asks Keep rolled back / Resume anyway with each failure and its reason, instead of committing the breakage silently (ADR-017 November 2026 "preflight resume" amendment; `apps/web/src/lib/historyResume.test.ts`, `ResumeHistoryDialog.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/feature-history.spec.ts`). Resuming one History row from its own Resume action now takes the same preflight: the candidate is rebuilt first and, when the row or a later feature that was building would fail, asks Keep suppressed / Resume anyway with each failure and its reason; a clean resume commits the same single command with the checked rebuild (ADR-017 November 2026 "preflight resume" amendment; `apps/web/src/lib/historyResume.test.ts`, `ResumeHistoryDialog.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/feature-history.spec.ts`). | [Holder contract](docs/plans/step-parameter-hammer-holder-plan.md). No private fixture publication; unsupported input must refuse honestly. |
| <a id="h02"></a>H02 | Partial | Characterize the reported 18–31 s first edit after reload: measure cold and warm runs on named hardware, identify the bottleneck and pin a justified regression budget, keeping preview timing separate from exact completion ([plan](docs/plans/edit-pipeline-performance.md)). **Delivered:** M5 Pro characterization finds edited-body measurement, not replay, is the bottleneck, with within-run budgets in `test/e2e/perf-holder-reload.spec.ts`; fixed Text no longer replays; publication determinism fixes; a lineage-runtime candidate cuts M5 p95 27.8% (≥20% gate) and passes the private 80-edit session; geometry-only and prepared face-drag previews, drag checkpoints and faster viewer installation; viewer CPU and first-hover fixes; checked Offset Face rim joining via Remus #1489 and paired 2026.1.104 (esaueng/remus#1496); 2.30x faster complex-fillet dragging (PR #1155); typed refusal past a fillet flat. **Remaining:** quiet-host and target-device timing, cold-join latency, whole-worker heap retention, the detached Text cache (unimplemented), sustained-session acceptance, unmet face-drag targets, orbit tails and the complex-fillet 2x target; the lineage-runtime candidate's adoption on `main` is not recorded. [Full history](docs/roadmap-history/h02.md). Face-offset and cylinder-diameter releases are accepted with a draft display: the commit keeps every gating check, refusal, exact measurement and volume, and the moved body is drawn with the drag tessellation until idle worker work swaps in the full-quality mesh; only the display-gap advisory waits for that refinement (`test/draft-display-release.test.ts`, `geometryWorker.test.ts`, `useGeometryWorker.test.tsx`). Local release → accepted on a private 32-feature filleted-union reproduction (top face, two runs of three): 291/282/292 and 317/286/530 → 117/85/92 and 119/92/98 ms ([edit sessions](docs/interactive-edit-sessions.md), PR #1364). Face-offset and cylinder-diameter releases: the commit adopts its own validation sync as the live result instead of a duplicate broadcast rebuild (`useGeometryWorker.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/filleted-union-face-offset.spec.ts`), and a release still validating can be taken again — set aside, never committed unvalidated, reused when the hand lands on the same value (`useDirectEditCommit.test.tsx`, `machine.test.ts`, retake e2e). Local release → live result on a private 32-feature filleted-union reproduction: top 488 → 293 ms, side −32 mm 602 → 381 ms; cylinder remains bound by the exact resize ([edit sessions](docs/interactive-edit-sessions.md), PR #1356). Multi-body edits no longer pay for unchanged bodies: the display-mesh closure verdict is remembered with the retained measurement, mesh buffers compare through `Uint32Array` views, and the worker publishes unchanged bodies by identity (`shareRetainedMeasurements`). Node replay of the worker path, edit to first geometry: 30 curved bodies 272 → 5.5 ms, 100 boxes 57 → 17 ms; the headless-SwiftShader browser edit was unchanged, bounded by main-thread frames (`test/exact-shared-measurements.test.ts`, `test/display-mesh-closure.test.ts`, [QA note](docs/qa/2026-11-12/multi-body-sync-overhead.md), PR #1366). Offset-face drags on native filleted histories stop replaying unchanged upstream work: carry-proposing previews and commit checks share history checkpoints with plain syncs while every hash-only fillet/chamfer pick resolves as stored (`test/carry-neutral-checkpoints.test.ts` proves a carry and a plain refusal of the same document are never served for each other); a checked sync keeps a terminal face edit's result next to its source when the schedule allows, so the broadcast after a commit replays nothing (`test/drag-source-checkpoint.test.ts`); and face-move drag frames mesh at the disposable drag tessellation with full quality at rest and on commit (`test/display-deflection-hold.test.ts`, PR #1350). Reload without replay: the geometry worker persists the history kernel as a Remus whole-kernel session snapshot (Remus #1664) with the last feature's build state, digests and projection signature, in its own IndexedDB database (never in backups, downloads, account sync or shares; only for the user's own local projects, never a shared link's model or a collaborator's project; bounded, retired with its project; keyed by content and app/Remus/kernel build). A reopened project restores it instead of replaying, publishes only after its projection matches the captured one (exact identities and lineage, measurements within 1e-9 relative) and replays on any mismatch or refusal; an idle, cancellable warm-up then rebuilds the ordinary checkpoint table in a second kernel (`test/kernel-session-snapshot.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/kernelSessionStore.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/worker/kernelSessionPersistence.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/worker/geometryWorkerSessions.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/kernelSessionPolicy.test.ts`, [persisted sessions](docs/history-cache-retention.md)). Local measurement on a 32-feature filleted native part (Linux, Chromium, Vulkan RX 6600, local Remus 2026.1.134 build): reload to input-ready 1.00–1.10 s with the session versus 3.96–4.03 s replaying in the same build; Node restore plus measure pass 135–176 ms versus 1.5–4.0 s replay; 3.7 MB snapshot, 15–17 ms idle write, about 3 s idle warm-up. A boss diameter release commits the resize its drag prepared instead of rebuilding it: a drag preview builds only the dragged edit display-only, so a prefix its first frame replays is checkpointed under the digests the checked release restores (`test/prepared-resize-release.test.ts`). Local release → accepted on the private 32-feature filleted-union part, first diameter release: 773/780 → 609/609 ms, the kernel's checked commit being 530–535 ms of it ([edit sessions](docs/interactive-edit-sessions.md), PR #1370). Reopen: picking a face on the saved projection while a reopened project is still replaying its history no longer supersedes that rebuild. The pick adds lineage demand; when the adapter's boolean-probe decisions (and so the rebuild result key) are unchanged, the newer broadcast joins the running build instead of cancelling it at the next feature boundary and replaying again from the last history checkpoint. A pick that does need a probe, or a newer version, still supersedes it (`apps/web/src/worker/geometryWorkerDemand.test.ts`). Local measurement on a 32-feature native part (Linux, Chromium with hardware GL) finds reload to first editable state about 6.7–7.0 s either way, because checkpoints usually limit the restart to the in-flight feature; one software-GL run lost about 3 s to the restart. The remaining reload cost is the full exact history replay; persisting kernel state needs a whole-kernel snapshot (topology, journal, handle namespace) that the pinned Remus does not expose. Correction, as of 2026-11-13: the earlier closing note that persisting kernel state needs a whole-kernel snapshot "that the pinned Remus does not expose" is superseded. A rounded primitive cylinder's diameter drag is instant again and edits the cylinder's radius instead of appending a local "Resize Cylinder" step. Two regressions, both visible only in full browser runs, had stopped it. First, the curved-surface outline's preview cache, counted against the mesh projection's 2 MB budget since #1290/#1333, pushed a Ø140 R2-rimmed cylinder to about 2.3 MB, so every drag frame went to an exact rebuild. That route now owns only a wall that reaches its cylinder across a boolean, where seam blends belong to the body. A cylinder whose own rim blends are all that separate it from its primitive keeps the source edit, with exact rim replay and parametric history (`cylinderPrimitiveAncestry.test.ts`). `cylinder-preview.spec.ts` passes all eight cases, and its radius drag case is now `@ci-smoke`. Slices: harness and material seam, lighting and material, edge ink, curved-surface outlines, stage, optional ambient occlusion. Follow-up, ambient occlusion: settled-frame contact shading in concave corners (`render/ambientOcclusion.ts`). It is computed once the view has rested for 150 ms, at half resolution (capped at 1.6 MP), with a tangent-plane estimator that leaves planar and convex faces untouched, and is applied inside the body shader so edge ink, overlays and the stage never darken. Moving frames cost nothing (measured: orbit CPU unchanged, a sub-microsecond camera check); the settle costs 7–19 ms of GPU once on an Intel UHD at an iPad Pro 12.9" canvas (2048 × 2732, 128 bodies). Slice 4, curved-surface outlines: cylinders, cones, spheres and fillets now outline where they turn away from the camera. Interior mesh edges of each curved face carry both neighbouring normals, and the fat-line vertex shader keeps only those straddling the view each frame, so the outline is GPU-side while orbiting and matches the B-rep edges' ink and width (`packages/viewport/src/render/silhouette.ts`, `silhouette.test.ts`). Slice 3, edge ink: idle edges are near-black (`#0b0d10`) at full opacity and 1.5 px, and tangent (fillet) rims rest at 0.42 instead of 0.18, which had all but vanished under the studio shading (`SELECTION_SEMANTICS.idle`). Project thumbnails take the same ink. Judged in the lookdev captures. iPad-class frame timing measured by proxy, not on a device (`perf-frame-timing.spec.ts`, `OZ_PERF=1`: desktop Chromium, 1366 × 1024 touch viewport at device pixel ratio 2, CDP CPU throttling ×1/×4/×6, Intel integrated GPU). The studio material and environment cost nothing measurable. The curved-surface outlines add 1–8 ms of GPU p95 per frame at ×1 and up to 9× the triangles drawn, and their candidate build stalls each curved body build for 22–320 ms (0.2–1.5 s at ×4–×6). The touch render budget absorbs most of the per-frame cost after the first gesture. Results and real-iPad instructions are in the plan's follow-up. Slice 1, no visual change: `OZ_LOOKDEV=1` builds the production renderer and `test/e2e/viewport-lookdev.spec.ts` captures five fixed scenes (`test/fixtures/lookdev-scenes.ts`) from three view-cube corners at device pixel ratio 2, canvas only, into `artifacts/lookdev/<label>`; `scripts/lookdev-compare.mjs` builds a click-to-flip before/after page (`test/lookdev-compare.test.ts`). `isBodyMaterial()` replaces the `MeshPhongMaterial` class checks that gated body buffer reuse and the selected-face fill, pinned in `bodyUpdate.test.ts`. Baseline captures taken locally at `3da524a4`. Face-offset push-in reads as one frosted slab over the part: while the ghost of the old part is shown, the moved face's selection fill is hidden, since seen through the ghost's differently lit top and sides it read as a misplaced blue panel standing off the part. The fill returns when the ghost clears (`ModelViewer.tsx`, held push and pull captured on the filleted union). Face-offset preview: the ghost of the part as it was reads as a frosted, outlined shape on the graphite stage (depth pre-pass hides its far side; stencil-masked, once-per-pixel edges never leak across the live solid). A pull outlines the old end on the extended part, and while the face is held the added material draws as ghost, solid again on release. Drag-time preview edges take the resting ink, so arcs no longer bead or dash before the commit (`bodyGhost.test.ts`, `bodyUpdate.test.ts`). Face-offset push-in no longer shows a black lid at the old face while the handle is held: the added-material veil now engages only when the displayed part reaches past the old face (a pull), and its cap carries the body's normals so it shades in the lit finish (`bodyGhost.test.ts`). Curved-outline candidates build 2–7× faster on the main thread (typed arrays and open-addressed hashing, bit-identical output; the rotational sleeve goes from 81 ms to 27 ms), and each build leaves an `oz:viewer.silhouettes` User Timing mark for the frame-timing probe (`silhouette.ts`, `silhouette.test.ts`). Curved-surface outlines are now picked on the CPU when the view changes and draw only the segments on the outline. Drawing every candidate each frame put 58,957 fat-line quads per frame on a filleted sleeve, which slowed `rotational-direct-edits` past its budget on CI's software rasterizer. The anchored-entity setting remains open. Keep attribution explicitly unknown when the solver cannot identify a culprit ([FreeCAD E03](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). [Production QA remediation](docs/plans/production-qa-fixes-2026-10-05.md) proposes the confirmed 5 October fixes; local regression and build evidence belong to its implementation PR, with production retest pending deployment. 14 October review follow-up UX-2 is delivered: rectangles and polygons drawn since S04 are lines whose corners carry point references, and every point-taking tool (Distance, Coincident, Midpoint, Symmetric, Point on curve, Lock, Disconnect) now refuses a snap without one by name instead of repeating its hint — a parametric rectangle's corners, centre or side midpoints and a parametric polygon's centre (redraw with the Rectangle or Polygon tool), a text origin, a midpoint, quadrant, intersection or the sketch origin; grid and axis-alignment snaps keep the hint (this PR; `sketch/constraints.test.ts` against the real snap generator, `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts`). Explicit conversion of legacy parametric shapes remains S04. Viewport relation glyphs (ZC-039) are delivered: every non-dimensional relation draws a glyph on the curve or point it holds (curve relations at a line's middle, a circle's 45° point or an arc's mid-sweep; point relations clustered per coincident point), relations the last solve named as conflicting are marked. A presentation-only `sketching.relationGlyphs` preference (Always · Selected · Hidden, default Selected, so a sketch shows the relations of the entity being worked on) sits in the sketch palette and Settings → Sketching; older clients and stored settings default it (this PR; `sketch/relationGlyphs.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchRelations.test.ts`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-relation-glyphs.spec.ts`). Research-audit Stage 2e completes the glyphs: each chip now stands off its geometry in screen space — a curve's chip on the side away from the sketch's centre (so a closed profile's chips sit outside it), a circle's outward, a point's chip in the widest gap between the curves meeting there — and chips claim space in a fixed anchor order (point chips first, then curve chips, by position), sliding along their curve or swinging round their point, then stepping out a ring, so none covers its own anchor, any other anchor or another chip, and none leaves the viewport; a chip with no room within that bounded search (a dense sketch zoomed far out) is hidden until zooming makes room. Screen directions are the projection's exact clip-space derivative, so they hold at any coordinate magnitude. The layout runs on every drawn frame from typed arrays and a screen grid, so it costs about linear time, allocates nothing and writes no style on a still frame; chips stay one CSS2D label per cluster. Because glyphs no longer sit on geometry they take clicks: a click selects that relation (shown on every chip it draws on; the entity selection stays, an armed pick tool gives way to Select), Delete removes it in one undoable edit before it would remove the entity, and Escape clears both in one press. A per-kind show/hide filter (`sketching.hiddenRelationGlyphs`, presentation only, no document schema change) sits in the sketch palette and Settings → Sketching; unknown kinds drop out and a save from an older client keeps the stored filter (this PR; `sketch/relationGlyphLayout.test.ts`, `sketch/relationGlyphs.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchRelations.test.ts`, `interaction/machine.test.ts`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/api-routes.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-relation-glyphs.spec.ts`). The anchored-entity rule (ZC-041) is delivered as decided: a relation now applies as it is added, in one undoable edit with its solved geometry, and its first pick stays put — a point pick holds that point, an object pick the whole object (a circle also keeps its radius) — so reversing the picks of Equal reverses which side keeps its size. Single-pick relations solve free. When holding the first pick makes the relation unsatisfiable the solve falls back to free and says so; when it cannot solve at all, nothing changes and the culprit is named, as for a driving dimension. A held solve's DOF counts held points as fixed, so it is not shown as the sketch status (this PR; `test/sketch-anchored-solve.test.ts` on the pinned kernel, `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts`). | Existing GCS detailed results; product S-3 and execution Slice D. |

| <a id="s05"></a>S05 | Partial | Trim/extend, offset, sketch fillet/chamfer, mirror and linear/circular patterns; each tool preserves constraint/refusal/undo contracts. Deliver each tool as a slice. Delivered: Fillet and Chamfer on the kernel's 2D operations for line pairs (Offset first shipped there for closed line loops), each one undoable transaction, with fillets writing coincidence, line-to-arc tangency and radius constraints (#333; `packages/kernel-adapter/src/sketch-2d-ops.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/edits.test.ts`). Delivered (research-audit Stage 2b, ZC-018/019/021/022): Trim, Mirror, Linear pattern and Circular pattern, app-side exact geometry folded with Offset into one "More modify tools" rail slot, each one undoable transaction. Trim cuts a line, arc or circle back to the nearest crossings either side of the click (deleting it when nothing crosses), keeps the entity's identity where it can, moves relations on a split's far end to the new piece, drops only those on removed points, and holds each cut end on its cutter. Mirror links the image with symmetric relations (coincident on the axis; equal radius for rounds); an on-axis joint's hold on the axis is kept only when a solve of the candidate on the pinned kernel shows it adds rank, so locks, coincidence chains and axes fixed through dimensions leave no redundant relation (`settleOptionalRelations`). Patterns take spacing and count, or count and total angle (rotated orientation), and tie each instance to the source with equal (plus parallel for linear lines). Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/modify.test.ts`, `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (solved on the pinned kernel), `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts`. Delivered (research-audit Stage 2b, ZC-014/015/024): Offset is now app-side exact geometry for a circle (radius r ± d; an inward distance that reaches the radius is refused with nothing changed), one line or arc, or, with Chain on, the run of lines and arcs joined end to end with the pick (closed or open, stopping at a branch; construction and regular curves walk separately, so a centre rectangle's diagonals never break its outline). A Miter or Round join is chosen on a strip beside the rail while Offset is armed (Miter default; a corner next to an arc is rounded); overlapping neighbours are trimmed to their crossing. Positive is outward for a closed loop, a circle or a lone arc, otherwise towards the side of the picked curve the click landed on. Every result is witnessed (no point nearer the source than the distance, no self-crossing) or refused. The copies join by coincidence and link back as optional relations (parallel, concentric, round-join centre on the source corner and tangent to its lines) kept only where a solve says they add rank. A 20 × 12 rectangle offset outward by 1 gives exactly 22 × 14 with Miter and radius-1 corner arcs with Round, in one undo step. Make construction / make regular: a Construction toggle on the entity editor flips one existing entity in one undo step; marking one side of a rectangle removes its profile and undo restores it. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/offset.test.ts`, `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (offset links solve without redundancy on the pinned kernel), `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts`. Open: extend, pattern by total extent, uniform orientation, a persistent pattern relation that also holds instance positions, multi-entity mirror/pattern/construction selections, a driving offset dimension, arc/circle inputs to Fillet/Chamfer, browser coverage of Fillet/Chamfer. Review follow-up: construction-toggle validation failures report the named refusal, preserve the last valid document, release the busy state and leave subsequent edits undoable (`test/e2e/sketch-delete-dependents.spec.ts`). Offset clearance now uses analytic minima over complete finite line/arc pairs; `offset.test.ts` covers narrow non-adjacent line and arc intrusions between the former sample locations while preserving valid smaller offsets. | S04 where composite identity is required; product S-5, design-review W15/phase 4. Research-audit Stage 0: deleting sketch geometry runs the same downstream rebuild check as an entity edit and refuses by name when a dependent feature would break (`test/e2e/sketch-delete-dependents.spec.ts`). |

Regression evidence: the draft-offset case in `DatumPlaneForm.test.tsx`, the slot refusal in `datumPlanes.test.ts`, the midplane case in `datum-face-repair.test.ts`, the split cases in `ai-datum-plane-ops.test.ts` (halves z 0–8/8–20, the back half mirrored by alias, a double split refused), the datum cases in `describe.test.ts` and `assistant.test.ts`, and the live-follow steps in `datum-plane-viewport.spec.ts`. Point-plane slice (#740, schema v25): Three points and Parallel at point planes from typed points or edge-end vertices by lineage, with collinear/coincident refusals; evidence in `datum-plane-point-constructors.test.ts`, `datum-point-planes` parity and e2e specs. Datum-axis slice (this PR, schema v28, additive): a `datum-axis` feature kind resolved once at its history position into a line published as `datumAxes`, with four constructors — through two points (typed or vertex), along a straight edge by lineage (end 0 → end 1), the axis of a cylinder or cone face by lineage (canonical, kernel-neutral sense) and where two planes meet (nA × nB through the point nearest A's origin) — each with Reverse direction; coincident points, curved/closed edges, non-revolution faces, missing sources and parallel planes are refused by name with no line published. Consumers hold the axis by id: a revolve's `axisRef` (axis must lie in the sketch plane, else refused), a circular pattern's `axisRef` (copies turn about the datum line) and a Move's `axisRotation`, created from the new Rotate about axis card and editable on every Move; the Revolve, Circular pattern and Move cards list earlier axes. An Axis tool (Create fold) opens a card that takes typed points, picked edge ends, a picked straight edge, a picked cylinder or two planes, commits only after an exact preflight shows the line resolved, and reopens from History; Items gains an Axes filter, History rows say how each axis is built, the viewport draws resolved axes as non-pickable dashed lines, deleting a used axis (or a plane an axis is built from) is refused with its dependents named, edits walk axis dependents, and a lost edge or face source offers "Re-pick source edge/face…". The Remus build and the OCCT parity reference share the construction rules (`packages/geometry`) and source resolvers (`face-attachment.ts`). Regression evidence: `datum-axis-geometry.test.ts` (two points, edge, canonical surface sense, XZ∩YZ = +Z, offset and oblique intersections on their analytic lines, parallel/antiparallel refusals on both sides of the tolerance, rotation about an axis, revolve-axis-in-plane rule), `datum-axis-feature.test.ts` (v28 round trip and v26 document unchanged, structural refusals, expression points, plane-intersection lines, parallel and coincident refusals, an edge axis following a block edit, a cylinder axis, planar-face and lost-source refusals by name, revolve annuli about axes at x = 0 and x = 20, the out-of-plane refusal, circular patterns about x = 10 (full ring and 90°), a Move turned 180° before its translation and reversed by Reverse direction, an axis edit moving its Move, delete refusals), `datum-axis-sources.test.ts`, the axis case in `boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, `test/parity/datum-axes.spec.ts` (revolves, an edge-axis pattern, a cylinder-axis Move and the parallel refusal in both kernels), `datumAxes.test.ts`, `DatumAxisForm.test.tsx`, `FeatureForms.datumAxes.test.tsx`, `datumAxisRig.test.ts` and `test/e2e/datum-axes.spec.ts` (a Two planes axis revolving a circle, a typed two-point axis turning a block 180° with Rotate about axis and ringing it with a circular pattern, an axis edit moving both, a coincident refusal creating nothing, undo). AI datum geometry slice (this PR): separately negotiated `add_datum_plane_v2` supports qualified face, typed-point and edge-vertex sources for all four shipped plane definitions; `add_datum_axis` supports all four shipped axis constructors. Full-definition plane/axis updates retain feature identity, and datum-axis revolve, circular-pattern and Move operations share the manual commands. Plane/axis aliases are typed and ordered; exact digest source proof, retained historical-source checks, own datum resolution and affected-consumer preflight refuse stale, ambiguous, forward and degenerate proposals atomically. Legacy plane/numeric operation contracts remain unchanged. Evidence: `packages/ai-contracts/src/datum-geometry.test.ts`, `packages/command-system/src/ai-datum-operations.test.ts`, `test/ai-datum-geometry-exact.test.ts`, `aiPatchPreflight.test.ts`, capability negotiation in `assistant.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/ai-datum-geometry.spec.ts` for Preview/Apply, refusal, undo/redo and reopen. Vertex-source recovery is separately in review (#802); broader reference constructors below remain open. Target catalogue from the comparison: planes offset from a face, midplane, through three points, on a curve at a point, perpendicular to an edge at a point, through an edge at an angle, parallel to a face at a point; axes through two points, at two planes' intersection, of a cylinder/cone, along an edge, perpendicular to a face at a point. Open: planes on a curve, perpendicular to an edge and through an edge at an angle; the axis perpendicular to a face at a point; datum points and coordinate systems. For supported attached datums, expose the defining source and qualified dependents through U06; upstream deletion or ambiguity keeps a named broken reference and explicit repair path. No proximity-based rebinding. Datum planes and axes draw in the "solid frame" look: a 32% fill and 2.5 px frame per plane, a 3 px line per axis, a dashed continuation through the part, a name chip on each, the selection hue when selected and a per-theme `--color-datum`; the world axes and origin planes are unchanged (`datumPlaneRig.test.ts`, `datumAxisRig.test.ts`, `stagePalette.test.ts`). Datum vertex repair (PR #802): History re-picks individual or all stored edge-vertex slots of three-point/parallel-at-point planes and two-point axes without changing datum identity, typed points, raw expressions, direction flags or untouched sources. Multiple lost slots stage before one exact-preflighted, undoable feature update. Invalid, ambiguous or closed-edge picks, degenerate geometry, failed affected consumers and stale/cancelled sessions cannot publish partial changes. Evidence: `test/datum-vertex-repair.test.ts`, `test/datum-vertex-repair-exact.test.ts` and `test/e2e/datum-vertex-repair.spec.ts`, covering downstream exact geometry, retry/cancellation, undo/replay/backup/reopen and compact controls. Boundary provenance recovery remains separate. | [Datum geometry plan](docs/plans/datum-geometry-plan.md); Product R-1; K05 for topology-derived references. Through-all slice (this PR): schema v20 adds `endCondition: 'through-all'` with a required `throughAllSide` (forward, backward or both) for a cut with exactly one explicit target; absent stays blind byte-for-byte, and new-body/add, missing target, symmetric or back-distance combinations are refused at entry. Each rebuild projects the target's exact bounds on the resolved sketch normal into a finite span and sweeps it through the existing cut, overlap and coplanar-retry path, so resizing or moving the target moves the cut and nothing derived is stored; a side that misses the target, or a deleted target, refuses by name. Intersect slice (ZC-060, this PR): schema v24 adds `operation: 'intersect'`, which keeps only the material the extrusion shares with its one explicit target and consumes that target; it is always explicit, never inferred, and through-all stays cut-only. The exact builder takes the typed exact intersect, refuses an extrusion that shares nothing (or would leave nothing) by name, and never pierces it; the OCCT parity reference models it as `common` with its target. The extrude card offers Intersect beside Add and Cut. Regression evidence: `solid-result-options.test.ts` (10 × 10 profile through a 10-deep block = 1000 by closed form, consumed target, refusals), `occt-solid-result-options.spec.ts` (both kernels against the same closed form), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx` and `solid-result-options.spec.ts` (2000 → 1000 with the block consumed). Each rebuild re-finds the face at the feature's history position and derives the distance from the sketch plane to it, so moving the face moves the extent and nothing derived is stored; a deleted, ambiguous, non-planar or no-longer-parallel face, or one lying on the sketch plane, refuses as "Target face is unresolved:" and the feature is skipped. That explicit state shows in the card and the history warning, and a History menu "Re-pick face…" (or the card's Re-pick face) replaces only the face through one validated, undoable edit. Draft tapers a one-sided blind or to-face extrusion about the sketch plane (positive narrows away from it): planar walls through the kernel draft, a single round wall as the exact cone loft between tapered cap circles; mixed curved/straight walls, a taper that closes the section, symmetric/two-sided and through-all refuse by name. A drafted extrude publishes hash-only references. The extrude card offers Extent → To face (any operation) with a viewport face pick, and a Draft angle; dragging the viewport arrow returns the extent to Distance, and the history row reads "To face · 2° draft". The OCCT parity reference resolves the same face through its own lineage and builds OCCT's drafted prism. Regression evidence: `extrude-to-face-draft.test.ts` (closed-form prismatoid, cone frustum, 19.1251 cap, moved/tilted/deleted face, re-pick repair, byte-identical load, edit command), `occt-extrude-options.spec.ts` (both kernels against the closed forms), `stage4-analytic-oracles.test.ts` (swept circle 80π, ruled loft 3066.67), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `featureSummary.test.ts` and `extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`. Remaining: multi-target and add through-all, to-next/offset-from-face, to-face on a slanted or curved face, start offsets and per-side draft. Size by module, circular pitch or diametral pitch (always per inch) with tooth count, pressure angle, profile shift, backlash, bore, rim and phase; keep-diameter or set-module fit on a cylinder; every face named from its kernel role (tip, flank, root, cap) so later fillets and holes survive edits; refusals (pointed tip, too few teeth, cylinder too small, partial span) and undercut advisories reported by the kernel by code. Remaining after the first slice: helical and herringbone teeth, bevel and worm gears, a meshing-pair helper that places a mate at the right center distance, and partial-span teeth. Straight bevel gears (schema v41): `gearType: 'bevel'` with the mate's tooth count and a shaft angle (default 90°), a Bevel choice in the Gear tool and edit from History, built through Remus `makeBevelGearDetailed`. Module, pressure angle, addendum and dedendum are heel values and the face width runs along the pitch cone; the teeth taper from the heel cap toward the cone apex. Flanks are the kernel's certified spherical-involute fits; profile shift, root fillet, helix and teeth on a cylinder are refused on a bevel gear. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (bevel validation and refusals; a 20:40 right-angle m 2 bevel gear's exact axial height `b·cos δ`, heel tip diameter, volume between the root and tip cone frusta less its bore, every face uniquely named; a face width reaching the apex refused as `gear:invalid-parameter`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (a bevel request carries its mate and drops spur-only fields) and `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (an untouched bevel edit writes back as stored; returning to an external gear clears the mate and shaft angle). First slice (schema v40): an additive `gear` feature kind with Gear (create) and Gear teeth (on a cylindrical face) in-place tools, edit from History, and the exact build through Remus `makeGearDetailed` / `cutGearTeethDetailed`. Helical external and internal gears, new or cut into a cylinder, take a helix angle (0–45°, normal-section sizing per ISO 21771) and a hand; a spur gear sends no helix fields, so it still builds on a kernel without helical gears, which refuses a helical spec rather than building a spur. Herringbone gears (`herringbone`, a Helix option) reverse the hand at mid-width; each half's faces are named `….start-half` / `….end-half`. Bevel and worm gears, the meshing-pair helper and partial-span teeth remain. Diametral pitch is read per inch in every unit system; tooth faces carry `gear.face.*` lineage from the kernel's roles; refusals surface as `gear:*` kernel refusals and undercut as an advisory. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (sizing conversions, frame, command validation and refusals, 20-tooth m 2 volume between root and tip discs with every face named, 2.2 in tip diameter for 20 teeth at P 10, undercut advisory, pointed-tip refusal, teeth on a Ø22 cylinder equal to the standalone m 1 gear's volume, cylinder-too-small refusal, helix-angle and hand validation, a 20° helical gear's transverse tip diameter, 40 named flanks and equal volumes for both hands, a herringbone gear's 80 half-named flanks and its volume equal to the single helix's, herringbone teeth on a Ø22 boss keeping its diameter with 80 half-named flanks) and `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (untouched edits of a new gear, of teeth on a body, of a helical and of a herringbone gear write back as stored; returning to spur clears the helix and hand, and to a single helix clears `herringbone`). Double-enveloping wheels (schema v45): `gearType: 'enveloping-wheel'`, its `teeth` its own teeth, `mateTeeth` the starts of the worm that drives it, `diameterFactor` and `wormLength` that worm's, `faceWidth` its face and `outsideDiameter` beside the throat; an Enveloping wheel choice in the Gear tool (Teeth, Starts, Diameter factor, Worm length, Outside diameter) and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeEnvelopingWormWheelDetailed`: the Hindley straight-sided tooth exactly in the mid-plane and, off it, straight sections fitted inside the tooth the worm would hob (sampled, not proven, and disclosed as approximate), creased along the mid-plane line; tips on the throat torus and the outside cylinder. Profile shift, root fillet, helix angle, a throat option and teeth on a cylinder are refused, and a worm length on any other gear. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (validation and thirteen refusals; an m 2, 30-tooth wheel for a single-start q 10, 24 long worm whose volume lies between its root and outside cylinders, every face uniquely named with both flank halves; a throat wider than the face refused as `gear:invalid-parameter`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (the request carries the teeth, the worm's starts as its mate, the worm length and outside diameter, without spur-only or throat fields; the count chip is its teeth), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/lib/featureSummary.test.ts`. Double-enveloping worms (schema v44): `gearType: 'enveloping-worm'`, its `teeth` the thread starts, `mateTeeth` the teeth of the wheel it wraps, `diameterFactor` its throat pitch diameter over the module and `faceWidth` its length; an Enveloping worm choice in the Gear tool (Starts, Teeth for the wheel, Diameter factor) and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeEnvelopingWormDetailed`: the Hindley worm whose thread is the surface the wheel's straight-sided teeth sweep, with exact torus lands and root floors and certified ruled flank patches. The wheel it envelops is not built. Profile shift, root fillet, helix angle and teeth on a cylinder are refused. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (validation and eleven refusals; an m 2, q 10 single-start worm for a 30-tooth wheel whose caps span its end tip diameter, volume between its throat root and end tip cylinders, every face uniquely named; a bore reaching the throat root refused as `gear:bore-too-large`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (the request carries the starts as teeth and the wheel's teeth as its mate, without spur-only or throat fields; the count chip is its starts), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/lib/featureSummary.test.ts`. Teeth cut along a cylinder leave each end cap as one face: Remus `cutGearTeethDetailed` returns the blank's disc and the toothed ring as two coplanar faces, which the adapter now unifies in place (`unifyFacesChecked`) and names `gear.face.cap.start\|end`. Dragging a gear cap edits the source cylinder's height, which re-cuts the teeth, instead of a local face move the kernel refuses (it cannot extend the NURBS involute flanks). The gear stays opaque to cylinder radius drags. Evidence: `test/gear-cap-height-drag.test.ts` (one named cap per end whose area is the prism's cross-section; top and bottom cap drags plan as cylinder height edits and rebuild with 40 named flanks and the opposite cap fixed; no radius ancestor through the gear). Gear parameter panel (shared by every in-place command): the bar's Options is a compact parameter sheet whose values are typed in place (numbers or expressions, arrow keys and label scrubbing) in their own units, so pressure, helix, phase and shaft angles read in degrees, profile shift in modules and diametral pitch per inch rather than in the document's length unit; a gear's rows show only where they apply (hand and herringbone once a helix angle is set, mate and shaft angle for a bevel, starts and diameter factor for a worm, rim for a ring, no sizing while teeth keep the cylinder's diameter), the tooth form folds away, and a summary gives the resulting module and pitch diameter. Changing the sizing keeps the tooth size (module 2 becomes 12.7 /in), the pitch chip edits the pitch the sizing reads, and the default diametral pitch matches the default module. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/panel.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/InPlaceCommandBar.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/gear.spec.ts` (Gear parameters). Standalone external and internal gear cap edits update the authored face width instead of requesting a local extension of the involute flanks. The near cap also shifts the gear's stored origin so the opposite end stays fixed. Routing requires a current named cap owned by that gear; teeth on a cylinder, bevel/worm gears, stale picks and locally edited results keep their existing routes. Expressions are retained and nonpositive widths are refused before rebuilding. `test/standalone-gear-height-drag.test.ts` covers 40 to 25 mm, unchanged sibling/topology/volume, undo/redo, reopening, STEP round-trip and the near cap of an X-axis gear. `faceOffsetPlan.test.ts` covers expressions, refusals and ancestry boundaries. `standalone-gear-height.spec.ts` exercises the actual Total keypad and checks the exported project still contains one gear feature with a 25 mm face width. Throated worm wheels (schema v43): `throated: true` on a worm wheel, with an optional `outsideDiameter`; a Throat choice (Plain or Throated) and an Outside diameter value under Options in the Gear tool, and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeWormWheelDetailed` with `throated`: across the throat, half way across the face, each tooth's tip is an exact torus patch about the worm's axis at its root clearance, and either side it stands at the outside diameter (kernel default: the throat diameter plus one module). The flanks stay the crossed helical wheel's, so contact is still at points. Throated is refused off a worm wheel, and an outside diameter without it. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (throated validation and refusals; an m 2, q 10, 30-tooth throated wheel at outside diameter 65 builds without warnings with its 65 mm outside diameter, volume bounds, `tooth[k].throat` and tip half faces all uniquely named, a plain wheel without throat faces; an outside diameter below the tips refused by the kernel), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (a throated wheel sends its throat and outside diameter, 0 leaves the default, a plain wheel or a worm sends neither), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (an untouched throated edit writes back as stored; making it plain clears both fields) and `apps/web/src/lib/featureSummary.test.ts`. Worms and worm wheels (schema v42): `gearType: 'worm'` (its `teeth` are the thread starts, its face width the length) and `gearType: 'worm-wheel'` (the worm's starts as `mateTeeth`), both with the worm's `diameterFactor`; Worm and Worm wheel choices in the Gear tool with Starts and Diameter factor under Options, and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeWormDetailed` (a cylindrical involute ZI worm, certified involute-helicoid flank fits) and `makeWormWheelDetailed` (the helical wheel of the worm's hand at helix angle equal to the lead angle, transverse module the worm's axial module). The pair is a crossed helical drive with point contact, not a hobbed throated wheel. Lead angles over 45° and profile shift, helix angle, herringbone and teeth on a cylinder are refused. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (worm and wheel validation and refusals; a single-start m 2, q 10 worm's exact length, tip diameter and a volume between two and three fifths of its thread annulus over the core, every face uniquely named; its wheel's tip diameter and volume bounds; a bore reaching the worm's root refused as `gear:bore-too-large`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (a worm sends its starts as teeth, a wheel as its mate, without spur-only fields; the worm's count chip is its starts) and `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (untouched worm and wheel edits write back as stored; returning to an external gear clears the worm fields). Remaining, as of 2026-11-12: the meshing-pair helper that places a mate at the right center distance, and partial-span teeth. This replaces the earlier "Bevel and worm gears, the meshing-pair helper and partial-span teeth remain" and "Remaining after the first slice" lists above: straight bevel gears (schema v41), worms and worm wheels (v42), throated worm wheels (v43) and double-enveloping worms and wheels (v44, v45) have merged, with the evidence recorded above. | Remus B262 (`makeGearDetailed`, `cutGearTeethDetailed` with face roles) in the pinned kernel; K05 face lineage. |

| <a id="i01"></a>I01 | Partial | Project model edges/silhouettes into sketches, then add spline/ellipse/slot/point entities as individual slices; add offsetting a body edge or edge chain into the active sketch; a projection carries a linked/unlinked choice; preserve source linkage and explicit broken-reference behavior. Expose the projection source and linked/independent role through U06. Repair after an upstream edit/deletion must use verified source identity and an explicit re-pick; preserve the last valid sketch until the replacement passes validation. Delivered: ellipse, elliptical arc, fit spline and control-point spline entities (research-audit Stage 2d, ZC-004/005/010; schema v27). Additive `ellipse` (centre, two radii, rotation, optional parameter range) and `spline` (fit or control method, degree 2/3, points, closed) sketch objects; stored documents are never rewritten. One geometry module derives every curve: a chord-length global interpolation (periodic when closed) and clamped uniform control splines, normalized to exact clamped B-splines with knot insertion for pieces, reversal and Bézier spans. Region analysis splits them against every kind (Newton-refined crossings plus endpoint projection; a tangential touch is not found) with exact Green areas, direction-canonical fingerprints and a self-intersection refusal; regions carry new `ellipse`/`spline` curves. The exact builder lifts true ellipse edges and the computed NURBS through `liftCurve2dToPlane`, cutting a clockwise full ellipse into quarters and a closed spline into halves because the pinned extrude chord-splits any other closed single edge into facets; the OCCT parity reference cuts the same pieces. Viewport: one curve rail slot (O ellipse, S spline, again steps the type) with Ellipse/Arc/Fit/Control tiles; ellipse is centre, axis end, other radius (then arc start and end); spline is clicks, Enter or a second click on the last point to finish, a click on the first point to close. GCS: an ellipse is a kernel ellipse held by its centre (coincident, lock, point-on-ellipse, concentric, equal radii); an open spline exposes its end points; interior points carry no relation. The entity card edits radii/rotation/arc range and each spline point; modify tools, revolve, sweep paths and the assistant refuse the new kinds by name. Sketch SVG writes elliptical-arc and per-span Q/C commands with an exact extent; sketch DXF (R12) refuses both by name, since R12 has no ELLIPSE or SPLINE. Evidence: `test/sketch-curve-entities.test.ts` (fit passes through its points with bit-exact ends, closed fit is C2 through its seam, πab regions rotated or not, half ellipse and quarter segment closed forms, circle hole in an ellipse, line crossing, spline closed/open/split areas, self-intersection, stable fingerprints), `test/sketch-curve-extrude.test.ts` (πab·h, taller-than-wide restatement, half and 220° arcs, ellipse and spline hole loops that a following box subtract accepts, closed spline as two walls), `test/parity/occt-reference/occt-curve-entities.spec.ts` (both kernels against the oracle with equal face counts), `test/sketch-curve-constraints.test.ts` (centre coincident, point on ellipse, concentric+equal, spline ends joined with interior points untouched, refusals, v27 round trip, sketch move), `test/sketch-svg-export.test.ts`, `test/sketch-dxf-export.test.ts`, web unit tests for the tools, card, rail and constraint picking, and `test/e2e/sketch-curve-tools.spec.ts`. Open: spline curve constraints (tangency, point-on-spline; Remus B75), trim/offset/mirror/pattern of the new curves, DXF R2000 ELLIPSE/SPLINE, slots, points and projection. Delivered: sketch projection with a linked/unlinked choice (research-audit Stage 2d, ZC-013; schema v33). An additive `projection` record on line, arc, circle and ellipse objects stores the source body and edge by schema-v5 lineage plus `linked` or `unlinked`; stored documents are never rewritten. The sketch rail's Project tool (U; its strip picks Linked or Unlinked) projects a picked model edge, or every boundary edge of a picked face, along the sketch normal. It follows the Construction toggle like every other drawing tool. Straight edges become lines. Circles and arcs become circles and arcs when parallel to the sketch, and exact ellipses and elliptical arcs when tilted. A line along the normal, a circle seen edge-on and any other curve kind are refused by name. Linked: at the sketch's history position the exact build re-finds each edge through verified lineage only (no hash match, no proximity). Every later feature in that build reads the re-projected geometry, so a downstream extrude follows an upstream resize in the same rebuild. The state is published as `derived.sketchProjections` (current, moved or broken). A move is written back to the object as a normalization with no undo step of its own. Line ends keep their names when an edge comes back reversed. The solver holds linked objects fixed, and drag, trim, fillet and chamfer refuse them until they are unlinked. Broken (source deleted, unavailable at the sketch's place in the history, or no longer the same curve kind): the stored geometry, which is the last valid projection, keeps building, and the sketch carries an advisory. Repair is an explicit Re-pick source on the entity card. It is validated by a rebuild before it commits and keeps the object id. Unlink instead keeps the geometry as an independent copy. A new linked pick is refused when its edge does not exist at the sketch's place in the history. Unlinked: copied once, ordinary editable geometry, source kept as provenance. U06: the Objects sketch row counts its projections and says when one is broken. Each projected entity is listed under it with its source feature and its linked/independent/broken role. The History row of a sketch with a broken projection is flagged. Evidence: `test/sketch-projection.test.ts` (exact kernel: a pick re-projects to exactly what it stored; a linked outline follows w 20→32 with the extrude at 32·10·3 in the same rebuild, then settles current after write-back; reversed line ends; unlinked never moves; a deleted source is broken with the last valid 25·10·3 plate still built and four advisories; an orphaned lineage refuses; history-position refusal; exact rim circle; v33 round trip and malformed-record refusals), `test/sketch-projection-geometry.test.ts` (closed-form line, circle, arc-side, tilted-circle r·cos θ ellipse and elliptical-arc oracles, refusals, tolerance and end-order rules), `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/projection.test.ts`, `ObjectsPanel.projections.test.tsx`, `HistoryTimeline.test.tsx`, `SketchEntityEditor.test.tsx`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`, `machine.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/sketch-projection.spec.ts` (projects a box's top face linked, widens the box from History and sees the stored outline follow, deletes the box and sees broken in Objects, History and the card, then unlinks). Remaining: silhouette projection of curved faces, spline and ellipse edge projection, and offsetting a body edge chain into a sketch. Constrained ordinary geometry now follows refreshed linked projections during exact history rebuilds. Every linked entity stays fixed, including current and broken links at their retained positions; only a successful solve publishes overrides. Unsatisfied relations refuse dependent features and affected exports without partial projection write-back (`test/sketch-projection-constraints.test.ts`). Cold rebuild, undo, replay, verified edge lineage, exact-volume and SVG/DXF export regressions cover the lifecycle. Solved ordinary neighbours also reach the sketch viewport through a project/version-qualified session view, so entity cards, endpoint snaps, point measurements and closed-profile picks agree with the rebuilt solid and exports. The view preserves authored values/history, is omitted from persistence, and is retained across geometry-preserving edits only after canonical geometry equivalence is proved. `test/e2e/sketch-projection-constraints.spec.ts` checks these reads after resize and reopen without a manual solve. Unlinking the last projection retains the solved shape in one undoable, replayable transaction; driver releases keep unchanged typed expressions. Exact regressions cover 576 volume after release, rollback without partial writes, undo/redo, cold replay/reopen/export, and an independent typed-height edit producing 672 volume. Saved linked projections demand Boolean lineage independently of transient selection, both through their producer reference and through a descendant source body. Exact-kernel cold reopen and cached rebuild regressions preserve the saved document, source reference and volume, while unlinked and unrelated controls retain lazy behavior (`boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, `sketch-projection.test.ts`). An explicit line re-pick retains the stored start/end orientation and construction role, preserving constrained endpoint identity (`apps/web/src/lib/sketch/projection.test.ts`). Projection source admission now requires the exact resolver's positive, canonical, hash-consistent edge reference; malformed references fail before native changes, account saves or HTTP/WebSocket collaboration storage. `sketch-projection-reference-validation.test.ts` covers zero/mismatched hashes, reversed witness endpoints, invalid closed axes and unsupported closed witnesses, unchanged storage, valid open/closed/null-axis controls and frozen ADR-011 hash values. The existing fingerprint algorithm and signature field order are shared with the kernel rather than changed; no valid document format, tolerance or schema version changes. Open circular edges published with only two tessellation endpoints are refused before a sketch copy is proposed: an endpoint cannot select the short versus complementary arc. Geometry and browser-pick regressions cover shallow parallel and tilted arcs in both directions, genuine interior-sample short-sweep controls, unchanged unlinked documents on refusal, and proven full circles with only closing endpoints. This narrows unsupported input; it does not fit or approximate an arc or change tolerances. CadPatch single and multi-profile extrusions resolve their sample points against the same project/version-qualified solved sketch view advertised in the assistant digest. Only reads use the session view; commands, authored expressions and history keep their original base. Append-only consumers and new or unchanged parameter bindings retain that qualified read snapshot. Qualified reads are tracked per sketch through its earlier feature, body, datum and linked-object dependencies, including in-place Moves. Renames and unrelated sketch/feature dimensions preserve them; edits to the sketch or its transitive geometry sources require an exact rebuild before profile selection. Existing parameter and structural edits conservatively require that rebuild. Exact-kernel regressions cover points outside the old outline, overlapping old/new outlines with independent area/volume oracles, repeated consumers, unchanged authored nodes, undo/redo/replay and wrong-project/old-version refusal controls. Consumers that only need a sketch id keep their existing command semantics and do not require profile-geometry reads. Compatibility controls retain multi-line command compilation and verify legacy single-circle extrusion/revolve with independent cylinder and torus volume formulas. | K05/K06 and sketch identity; product S-6/S-7, design B5/phase 6; Remus B75 for spline solver constraints (the pinned GCS already has ellipse entities) and 4.5/7.4 for projection and imprint (C8).
