| <a id="i05"></a>I05 | Partial | Reference images: place PNG/JPG (first PDF page through the existing rasterizer) on a plane or face with a gizmo for scale/move/rotate and an opacity slider, listed as an item, usable as a tracing underlay in sketches and as a split tool later. Stored as an artifact, not in the document body. Research-audit Stage 6 slice (this PR, schema v32): the Create fold's Image tool places a PNG or JPEG (sniffed by content, at most 20 MB and 8192 px a side) on Top (XY), or on the flat face selected when it opens; its card moves it to any principal plane (with offset), a resolved datum plane (followed by id) or the selected face (followed by lineage, or a fixed frame for a face without identity), sets position U/V, rotation, width (height follows the aspect) and opacity as a live draft applied in one undoable edit, and scales it from two points picked on the picture and a known distance. Objects lists images with show/hide (view state, like datums), an Images filter and name search. The record lives in `ProjectDocument.referenceImages` and holds only a SHA-256 reference: the bytes go to the content-addressed source blob store and a `reference-image` artifact (PNG/JPEG only, checked at upload-session creation and finalize), never inline, and a browser without blob storage is refused. Images are never features or bodies, are excluded from the geometry rebuild key, share snapshots and diagnostics, are never raycast and sit outside the PNG export's allowlist; STEP/mesh/drawing exports cannot see them. Backups carry the picture as a source and refuse one without it; blob cleanup keeps every picture the document or its undo history names. A device-only picture (local project, or a failed upload) counts as a local-only source and is archived, with its id rewritten through history, by the next account save or local-project adoption; a duplicate or branch caches the source's pictures and holds them under device-local ids instead of depending on the source project's artifact, and an account duplicate uploads them into the new project and stores the rewritten copy before reporting it made. An insert is validated in full (including the 64-image cap) before any byte is stored or uploaded, and a texture that could not load retries when the image's artifact id changes. Regression evidence: `test/reference-images.test.ts` (schema, commands, undo/replay, validation at load and the account edge, history snapshots, geometry key, share/diagnostics, upload content types, two-point scale), `referenceImageDisplay.test.ts`, `referenceImageRig.test.ts`, `referenceImageInsert.test.ts`, `ReferenceImageForm.test.tsx`, `ObjectsPanel.images.test.tsx`, `projectBackupTransfer.test.ts` and `test/e2e/reference-images.spec.ts`. Remaining: a viewport gizmo for move/rotate/scale (fields only today), PDF first-page import, sketch tracing snaps and split-by-image, share-link delivery of pictures, and a server-side artifact copy for account duplicates (today the duplicating device uploads the pictures). | U06 items; the pdf.js rasterizer already used for assistant ingestion; [comparison §2.9](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). |

## File interoperability

This lane has its own bounded sequence within the delivery milestones above:
complete [D04](#d04) DXF sketch exchange, [I07](#i07) SVG and [I06](#i06)
IGES, alongside [I02](#i02) STEP fidelity. Qualify [I10](#i10) before selecting
native-CAD implementations; the first package is [I11](#i11) Parasolid,
[I12](#i12) ACIS, [I13](#i13) Rhino and [I14](#i14) SolidWorks part import.
Inventor/JT follow demonstrated demand; Creo/NX/CATIA/Solid Edge are retained
as later targets. [I08](#i08) PLY export is a small independent slice; PDF
remains [D01](#d01), and [I09](#i09) USDZ is deferred visualization work.

Import means qualified geometry and supported metadata, not recovered source
feature history. Per-format direction/version claims require fixtures; native
assembly references must resolve explicitly, and mesh-only input cannot become
an exact solid merely by translation. Details and source evidence live in the
[format specification](docs/plans/file-format-interoperability.md); this table
is the only delivery-status ledger. Existing I04 owns export controls; I07 owns
SVG geometry, replacing the previously unnamed SVG slice in D04/I04.

Reject or explicitly classify open/unsupported geometry. | Remus O5.7 ([#883](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/883)); pinned IO APIs are not qualification. Delivered (export half, ZC-190, with the ZC-192 view PNG): File › Export image… (also in the command palette) writes one saved sketch as SVG through the D04 sketch extraction, so it refuses the same sketches by the same names (text, construction-only, stale attachment…); lines, circles and arcs stay exact `<line>`/`<circle>`/elliptical-arc `<path>` elements (never sampled) in DOCUMENT units, with v negated for SVG's y-down axis, a viewBox over the exact curve extent (arc axis extremes included) plus half a 0.25 mm stroke, and a physical width/height that opens at 1:1 (metres stated in millimetres); construction geometry is left out and a transparent-background option drops the white backing rect. SVG review follow-up: normalize finite arc starting angles before bounded quadrant checks, and retain nearly full turns with two arcs when their formatted endpoints coincide. Browser downloads prove both cases remain visible. PNG rendering preserves per-material tone mapping, including the unmodified backdrop; a native-resolution browser comparison fails by 16 colour steps before the fix and matches within one rounding step afterwards. A second-sketch History selection defaults the dialog to that sketch and downloads its distinct geometry; the browser regression fails against the prior default. Selected-body images also exclude uncommitted Move-copy projections; a viewport regression checks capture scope and restores their visibility afterwards. PNG capture also suspends sketch/profile selection and hover styling, including profile boundaries and pick markers, and restores the exact live styles after success or renderer failure. The same dialog renders the current view as a PNG at a chosen size (View, 2× view, 1920×1080, 3840×2160 or typed, up to 8192 px a side and 40 MP) offscreen on the live renderer — MSAA render target, then the renderer's own tone mapping and sRGB encoding with alpha un-premultiplied — and restores the camera, visibility and clear state in the same synchronous call, so the live canvas never presents a capture frame; a wider/narrower image widens the frame rather than cropping it, gizmos/rigs/measurements and selection glow are left out, the grid is optional, and the background can be transparent. Scope follows #707: a selected body draws alone, otherwise the visible bodies with hidden ones counted as left out. The PNG is written by an in-app encoder (8-bit RGBA, IHDR/IDAT/IEND only, so no metadata) to keep straight alpha exact. Desktop saves accept `.svg`/`.png`. ## 3. Drawings

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / legacy item |

| <a id="d01"></a>D01 | Partial | Isolated-body drawing entry (this PR): first entry opens the unique saved sheet matching the visible result bodies, including after a cold rebuild; returning from Build remembers the chosen sheet within the project session. The properties panel names the sheet bodies and explains that Hide/Isolate does not rebind saved views. Canonical drawing and section requests use idle boolean-lineage demand, avoiding an unnecessary probe upgrade and model replay; modeling consumers still demand full lineage when needed. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` and `test/boolean-evolution-demand.test.ts` cover source choice, retained selection/projections, absence of extra probes, canonical output and a later explicit lineage demand. [Drawing MVP design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md) records the contract and this slice's decisions; Peter chose the Draw workspace mode and accepted both departures (2026-10-19): the fingerprint fence with explicit Update instead of checkpoint replay, and worker-side visibility against the display tolerance, labelled approximate, instead of `projectEdges`. Canonical drawing tessellation (follow-up to #753): views no longer project from the viewport's display mesh, which holds an earlier deflection through edits within 25 % of it while a cold reload re-meshes at the nominal value (so a sheet could read ready after a reload yet export different bytes). A `drawing-tessellation` geometry-worker request meshes each sheet body at its nominal display deflection for its own exact bounds and puts faces, triangles, vertices and edge polylines in an order that depends only on their coordinates, so checkpoint replay, a full rebuild and a cold reload give the same arrays; the Draw workspace caches it per fingerprint, so a hidden-line toggle does not mesh again. The fingerprint is unchanged (no tessellation signature, existing sheets stay ready): the canonical mesh is a function of the exact geometry it already fences. The display path is untouched; a drawing request leaves the next sync's checkpoint restore and measurement reuse unchanged. Evidence: `test/drawing-canonical-tessellation.test.ts` (a sheet exported in a session after a held-deflection parameter edit and after a checkpoint-replayed edit, then rebuilt cold in a fresh kernel: byte-identical PDF and SVG, while the display-mesh input demonstrably differs; refusal of a consumed body; permutation invariance), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (projects from the requested tessellation, meshed once across a hidden-line toggle; a refusal shows after two retries), and `test/e2e/drawings.spec.ts` "exports the same bytes after a reload re-meshes the model" (R20→R21 disc, export, browser reload, export: identical bytes; fails when fed display meshes). Foundation slice delivered (research-audit Stage 7, ZC-139–142, 164; schema v30): `ProjectDocument.drawings` holds sheets with ISO A4–A0 or ANSI A–D paper and orientation, a shared integer scale, first/third projection angle and title-block text (title, drawing number, revision, author, date; scale, sheet, units and projection fill from the sheet), and per-view orientation (six principal views or the true isometric), bodies, paper-mm centre, hidden-line toggle and an aligned base for projected views. A new **Draw** workspace mode (sheets in a left column, the sheet canvas over the stage with the viewer kept mounted, properties on the right; lazy chunk) makes a four-view starter scaled to fit (front base, top and side folded on the projection angle's sides, isometric free), moves views by drag, fields or arrow keys with projected views kept on their base's row/column, and exports a sheet as dependency-free vector PDF (base-14 Helvetica, WinAnsi; other characters become "?") or SVG in millimetres, byte-stable across reload. Projection runs in its own worker from the canonical drawing tessellation: kernel-sampled edge polylines plus curved-face silhouettes, occlusion against every body's triangles (approximate to the display tolerance) — the pinned `projectEdges` tests occlusion only locally and draws no silhouettes, recorded as a diagnostic test. Model fence: each sheet stores a fingerprint of its bodies' persistent topology hashes and extents; an edited model shows a stale state, draws outlines only and refuses export until an explicit Update (undoable). Evidence: `test/drawing-projection.test.ts` (box principal-view bounds, isometric far-corner hidden runs, cylinder silhouettes, U-part and two-body occlusion, drilled-plate bore), `test/drawing-sheets.test.ts` (schema, validation on load and at the account edge, commands with undo/redo/replay, alignment, fingerprint, 1:2 and inch transform, starter layout, PDF/SVG structure and reload byte-stability), `test/e2e/drawings.spec.ts`. Open: checkpoint-snapshot replay of an old model (the fence refuses instead), per-view scale and custom view angles, exact silhouettes (Remus ask, ZC-145), drawing on share links; title-block layouts and D02 annotations stay with D02. Production QA drawing fixes: views are flagged by their whole footprint and export is refused while one runs off the paper, with an undoable "Fit views to sheet"; the composed page and PDF are clipped to the paper; the title block prints a full ISO date; ⌀ and other common symbols print as Latin-1 stand-ins with a warning for the rest; the sheet zooms and pans; view labels are documented as editor-only (`test/drawing-sheets.test.ts`, `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx`). Draw workspace "paper first" layout (design direction A, chosen 2026-10-31): sheet tabs, the sheet's sync state, zoom and Export PDF/SVG over the sheet in place of the left sheet column and the panel-bottom export; annotation tools on a floating icon rail beside the paper with one-key shortcuts (H, V, A, R, D, G, N; Esc backs out of a tool, then a selection); a contextual properties panel (sheet settings, or the selected view's or annotation's); editor view names at a readable on-screen size; while the model rebuilds, the sheet keeps its last drawing faded, with the rebuild's feature progress, and stays unexportable; and an editor-only dark paper toggle (per device) that never reaches PDF or SVG. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (faded last drawing while rebuilding, tool keys taken ahead of the workspace shortcuts and never from a field, dark paper remembered) and the drawing e2e specs updated for the moved controls. A sheet whose model changed keeps its last drawing (views, dimensions on that model, export) for the session behind one compact "Model changed · Update" pill instead of a full-width banner and outlines; undoing an Update restores it (`DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` "keeps the earlier drawing usable after a model change until Update", `drawings.spec.ts`, `drawing-dimensions.spec.ts`). Sheet scales and Auto scale: the scale list gains the ISO 5455 enlargements 50:1 and 20:1 and the common intermediates 4:1, 1:4, 1:8, 1:25 and 1:250 (the starter and "Fit views to sheet" pick from the same list, so they can land on these). A new undoable **Auto scale** action lays the sheet's views out again as an evenly spaced grid at the largest preset at which the grid fits above the title block: views whose centres share a row or column keep sharing it, so projected views stay aligned, but the gaps are set afresh, so views that overlap or sprawl after a scale picked by hand come out clean, and a sheet shrunk by hand grows back to fill the paper. Evidence: `test/drawing-sheets.test.ts` ("auto scales views to the largest preset, spaced evenly in a grid"), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (preset list, one-command Auto scale with status). Sheet tabs scale to many sheets: one row that scrolls sideways (edge fades mark hidden tabs, a plain wheel scrolls it, the active tab is kept in view) instead of wrapping into rows that pushed the zoom and export controls down; an "All sheets" list once the row overflows (number, name, paper, scale, view and note counts, opened at the active sheet); the paper tag shown only when sheets differ in paper; arrow keys, Home and End move between tabs and only the active tab is a Tab stop. Evidence: `DrawingSheetTabs.test.tsx` (paper tag only on mixed paper, arrow-key selection, list only when overflowing). | Design sheet/view document model first; product D-1. No dependency on finishing every M-row. |
| <a id="d02"></a>D02 | Partial | Associative drawing dimensions, centerlines, notes, title blocks and hole callouts; model edits update or visibly invalidate annotations. Comparison decisions: refresh on an explicit Update command with visible staleness is the accepted minimum; the dimension set is line length, point-to-point, point-to-line, line-to-line, arc angle, 3-point angle, line-to-line angle, radius, diameter and min/max distance, each with prefix/suffix and a tolerance (symmetrical, deviation, limits, basic); geometry annotations are 2-point, 2-line, 3-point and circular centerlines, center marks and intersection marks that also serve as dimension references; notes and sheet images; units, angle format, precision, decimal separator and per-class line widths are drawing properties. First handoff slice: place a dimension on a D01 view, preserve its placement/formatting through offline reopen, and update or visibly invalidate it after an explicit source rebind before PDF export. Invalid annotation references retain presentation state and offer explicit qualified repair/re-pick, never nearest-edge rebinding. Title-block fields (title, units, scale, projection angle, sheet size, date) fill automatically until edited. Layouts, fields and repair remain implementation scope, not delivered design evidence ([FreeCAD E06](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). First slice delivered (research-audit Stage 7, ZC-146/163; schema v31; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#implementation-decisions-d02-annotations-schema-v31)): `DrawingSheet.annotations` holds horizontal, vertical and aligned two-point dimensions, radius, diameter and angle dimensions, and text notes on the paper or in a view with an optional leader. Each stores only the edges it measures (body id, ADR-011 hash and ADR-013 persistent reference, resolved lineage-first through the fail-closed `resolveEdge` ladder) and its placement relative to its view, never a value: values are read from exact edge vertices and published circle data each time a ready sheet is drawn, so a stale sheet shows none until its explicit Update, and a parameter-widened box reads its new width after Update. A reference that resolves to no edge or to several, a body no longer in the view, a circle now edge-on or edges now parallel keep the annotation in place, marked broken with a reason; export is refused until it is re-picked (placement and format kept) or deleted, and nothing rebinds to a nearby edge. Picking offers only edges the view draws, prefers the nearer of coincident edges and refuses an edge whose identity another shares; aligned spans and angles that slope into a principal view, and every length or angle on the isometric view, are refused rather than read foreshortened (horizontal and vertical spans along a model axis stay exact). Dimension strokes, filled arrowheads and rotated text go through the one page model into the preview, PDF and SVG; a sheet without annotations exports byte-identical to D01. Evidence: `test/drawing-annotations.test.ts` (schema pin and additivity, refusals on load and at the account edge, commands with undo/redo/replay, view delete, horizontal/vertical/aligned/angle/radius/diameter values on real kernel bodies, lineage survival across a parameter edit, broken reasons, picking, iso refusal, layout, PDF/SVG text and reload byte-stability), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (drawn value, broken state with export refusal and Re-pick, stale sheet, keyboard move/delete, Escape) and `test/e2e/drawing-dimensions.spec.ts` (pick and place horizontal, vertical and leader note on a box; delete/undo; SVG/PDF contents; widen → stale with no values → Update → new value; reload). Open: tolerances, prefix/suffix, centrelines/centre marks as references, point-to-line and min/max distances, title-block layouts, hole callouts and drawing-level unit/precision properties. Finishing-body repair (PR #890): an explicit, undoable sheet Update follows unique one-to-one hole/fillet/chamfer/shell/draft/offset results, retaining subshape witnesses, annotation placement and export guards. Additive Thicken results are excluded. Missing, ambiguous and removed edges still require repair. Draw-mode revision saves use the drawing permission guard; new-sheet controls wait for ready geometry. Documents use schema-v39 (built on v38) to fence replay from older clients that would ignore body replacements. Tests: `test/drawing-body-succession.test.ts`, `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx`, `design-review-fixes.spec.ts`. Production verification remains pending. The broken-annotation warning offers **Delete all broken**, removing only unresolved dimensions and note leaders on the current sheet in one undoable transaction. Healthy annotations and other sheets are preserved. Cleanup waits for every view to finish drawing and is unavailable during rebuilds, read-only access or a proposal preview, so temporary section/leader resolution failures cannot remove saved annotations. Stable lineage and unique geometry hashes continue to update dimensions; removed or ambiguous geometry still requires an explicit re-pick rather than a guessed substitute. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/drawing-dimensions.spec.ts`. | D01, dimension semantics from S01/S02; M01 for hole metadata. Product D-2. |
| <a id="d03"></a>D03 | Partial | Section/detail views with reliable exact curves, clipping/cropping and correct scale: a section from a drawn line with the result dragged into place along its arrow; a detail from a drawn circle with its own scale and hidden-line toggle. Section view slice (schema v47, [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#implementation-decisions-d03-section-views-schema-v47)): a section line dragged across a front, top or side view (snapped to the view's middle or a circle's centre) makes a section view, moved into place along its arrows and kept aligned with its source on the projection angle's side. `DrawingView.section` stores only the source, a label and the plane's depth. Each body is cut by an exact half-space boolean in the geometry worker and projected like any view, so the cut outline is kernel-sampled exact edges; cut faces are hatched, hidden lines follow the view's toggle, and the cutting-plane line (chain, thick ends, arrows, letters) and "SECTION A-A" caption go through the one page model to preview, PDF and SVG. Where the pinned kernel refuses the exact cut (most planes through a cylindrical hole in a box), the body is cut from its tessellation and labelled approximate, without dimensionable cut edges; a plane that cuts nothing is refused on the view and blocks export. Sections follow the sheet's explicit Update and reopen from the saved document. D02 dimensions resolve on the cut body's own edges: a blind Ø8 bore 18 deep, cut down its axis, dimensions its floor at 8 and its depth at 18, and a moved floor breaks the dimension rather than rebinding it. Evidence: `test/drawing-sections.test.ts` (schema refusals, commands, exact cut of the bored cylinder with hatch area 456 mm², floor and depth dimensions, broken reference, refused plane, tessellation fallback on the bored plate), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (hatched section with line and caption, refusal without retry, drag-and-click creation) and `test/e2e/drawing-sections.spec.ts` (bored plate: section across the bore, Ø12 dimension on the section's circle, SVG/PDF contents, reload). | <a id="d04"></a>D04 | Partial | Whole-sketch/flattened-view DXF export and DXF sketch import with units/entity fidelity and malformed-input tests. Delivered slices: exact section curves export as DXF (#342) and the writer refuses non-finite/oversized coordinates and zero-wire input by name (#349; `test/dxf-writer.test.ts`). The first whole-sketch export contract is now source-grounded in [`docs/plans/sketch-dxf-export-plan.md`](docs/plans/sketch-dxf-export-plan.md): exact local-plane LINE/CIRCLE/ARC output, exact rectangle/polygon line expansion, millimetre conversion, construction exclusion, and fail-closed unsupported/text/plane cases. SVG import/export is owned by I07. Runtime whole-sketch export is delivered in the adapter and worker: `exportSketchDxf` lowers one saved sketch to exact local-plane LINE/CIRCLE/ARC in millimetres through the existing `writeDxf`, expands rectangles/polygons to their edge lines, excludes construction geometry, and refuses by name (`sketch-not-found`, `parameters-invalid`, `stale-plane-attachment`, `invalid-plane-frame`, `unsupported-object`, `invalid-geometry`, `construction-only`, `writer-refused`) (this PR; `test/sketch-dxf-export.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketchDxf.test.ts`). DXF sketch import (this PR): an ASCII DXF imports through Import CAD files or a drop as one undoable sketch on Top (XY) named after the file. LINE, CIRCLE and ARC map exactly; LWPOLYLINE/POLYLINE segments become lines, and their bulges the arcs they describe; `$INSUNITS` (inch, foot, mm, cm, m and smaller units) converts into the document units, and a file declaring none is read in the document units and says so. Annotations and blocks (TEXT, MTEXT, DIMENSION, HATCH, INSERT…) are left out and counted, curves a sketch has no exact object for (ELLIPSE, SPLINE, 3D polylines, entities off the XY plane) are refused and counted, and binary DXF, a missing ENTITIES section, malformed group codes, unknown units, more than 2,000 curves or over 20 MB refuse the file by name. Review hardening (#723): reject every nonzero elevation (including legacy vertices), refuse curve/spline-fit parent flags and generated fitted/control vertices before expansion, and require positive CIRCLE/ARC radii. Regression evidence: `test/dxf-import.test.ts` (round trip through the sketch DXF writer, bulge direction, a slot closing into one region, units, counts, refusals, signed/tiny elevations, fitted flags/vertices and zero/negative radii) and `dxf-sketch-import.spec.ts` (inch slot imported and extruded). The sketch menu export entry, flattened views, Bézier/ellipse/spline import and a user choice of units for unitless files remain open. | Sketch entity support; D01 only for drawing-view export. Product D-4; can precede D01 for sketch-only slices. |
| <a id="d05"></a>D05 | Deferred | Drawing dimension audit → reviewed parameter overrides without a fresh proposal; retain measured-vs-inferred provenance. | D02/F03; product D-5 and former TODO AI follow-up. |
| <a id="d06"></a>D06 | Partial | Auto-dimensioning: one request (a Draw button, or the assistant) places a complete, non-redundant dimension set — every feature size and location stated once from a datum, no closed chains or derived values, each in a view that sees it true size. A deterministic generator and checker own the guarantee; the assistant chooses style and scope and its proposal is shown only once the checker passes. Design intent (history, else recognized features with virtual sharps) before raw geometry. [Design](docs/plans/drawing-auto-dimensioning-plan.md): slices A checker → B schema (counts, Ø on linear, silhouette/virtual-sharp anchors, chamfer, reference, provenance) → C button → D imports → E assistant → F sections. Slice E, first half: asked from Draw, the assistant proposes dimensions and notes on the open sheet. `add_drawing_annotations` and `remove_drawing_annotations` (strict schema, nullable fields) name edges only by the refs the sheet digest lists per drawn view (`v1:e4`, with page-mm ends, centres and face-on circles); digest validation grounds each ref to its model edge, and the app adds the lineage a pick would store. A proposal is previewed on the sheet only once every annotation resolves and the dimension check finds nothing repeated; missing sizes are reported in the digest's audit, not refused. It applies as one undoable step; model edits stay out of Draw. Consent copy v2 lists `digest.drawing`. The deterministic generator (slice C) plugs in at `preflightDrawingPatch`. Evidence: `packages/ai-contracts/src/drawing-annotations.test.ts`, `test/drawing-assistant-operations.test.ts` (real kernel plate: digest, two dimensions applied and undone as one step, replay, refusals) and `AssistantPanel.drawing.test.tsx` (fake provider proposes two dimensions). Slice C, the Auto-dimension command: Draw's Dimension check has an Auto-dimension button that, with no assistant, previews on the sheet exactly the dimensions the check finds missing and adds them as one undoable command on Apply. It reads the checker's own requirement model: one radius or diameter per unstated size group, and per model axis one baseline dimension from the lowest datum position to each group of positions not yet linked, so it closes no chain and states nothing derived. Each goes in a principal view that sees it true size and visible, preferring the view holding the body's other dimensions (never the isometric view), on edges whose identity no other edge shares. Linear dimensions stack outside their view, shortest nearest the part; nothing overlaps text, other views or the title block, and all stays on the paper. Existing dimensions are kept and counted; what the check cannot place yet is skipped and reported. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (on real kernel bodies — box, stepped part, 2 × 2 hole plate, bolt-circle flange, rounded corner, turned foot: after Apply the check reports zero missing and zero repeated, every layout is clash-free, a rerun adds nothing, a user's dimension is kept) and `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (preview, Apply as one transaction, Escape cancels, disabled for viewers). Slice A, the dimension check: Draw's panel reports, for a sheet that matches the model, which sizes and positions its dimensions state, which are missing, which are repeated (a second dimension on one size group, a closed chain such as 50 = 41 + 9, or a span to a blend's tangent the radius already fixes) and which no D02 dimension can place yet (revolved blend radii, chamfers and tapers, bolt-circle pitch diameters, positions only closed circles mark). Positions per model axis are planar faces square to it and hole/boss axes; blends tangent to a face are positioned by their radius; three or more equal holes equidistant from a turned axis are one bolt circle, not per-hole X/Y positions. Read from the current model like every dimension; nothing stored, no schema change. Evidence: `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts` (on real kernel bodies: the turned foot of the design, a stepped part's closed chain, a plate's hole located by its centre, a bolt circle, a rounded corner's derived tangent) and `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (missing list on a ready sheet; a repeated dimension selects from the check). Slice B, schema v48 (builds on v47, D03 section views): Draw annotations can now dimension a turned or chamfered part. A rim seen side-on is picked as its silhouette end (`silhouette` point), two face outlines as their virtual sharp (`virtual-sharp` point, from the kernel's exact planes, cylinders and cones), and a cone or edge-on plane as a Chamfer dimension (`size × angle`, through blends). Radius, diameter and chamfer dimensions take a count (`4×`), a linear span a Ø prefix, any dimension a reference flag (brackets), and any annotation generated provenance. Older clients refuse the document; load, save and the account edge refuse malformed additions by name; nothing stores a value. The checker now counts rim silhouettes as position marks, a symmetric Ø span as a diameter or bolt-circle pitch, and chamfer dimensions as chamfer groups: the turned foot's Z positions, both chamfers and a bolt circle's pitch move from "can't dimension yet" to satisfiable, and a seven-dimension foot sheet passes with nothing missing or redundant. Auto-dimension (slice C) ends position spans on rim silhouettes, so it now places the foot's heights, and reports chamfers and pitch diameters it leaves to the person. Evidence: `test/drawing-dimension-schema.test.ts` (real kernel bodies: the foot as a revolved profile, a planar chamfer, a two-hole plate; refusals; SVG/PDF byte-stable across a reload; picking), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts`, and the e2e in `test/e2e/drawing-dimensions.spec.ts` picking a cylinder's rims for its height and a reference Ø. Slice D, schema v49 (builds on v48, slice B): Auto-dimension dimensions a history-less STEP import from the features its faces show. The plan's turned foot, exported through the kernel and re-imported (11 analytic faces, no history), gets exactly the plan's eight dimensions: Ø40, Ø33, Ø25, 2× 4 × 45°, 3× R4, 9, 29 and 50, with no blend tangent height, no Ø32 and no 3.5. The checker groups a step chamfer that runs into a smaller diameter with an end chamfer of equal angle on the same diameter, and locates it at the corner it cut (a new `chamfer-corner` point, the 29). It also states revolved blends from their torus face seen side-on (a radius dimension on a face, `3× R4`), and calls blend tangent heights, a chamfer's own sharps and the contact Ø32 derived. Auto-dimension now places counted chamfer and blend dimensions and counted diameters, and gives every dimension it places provenance naming its requirement. On an import, provenance marks a span to a face no feature explains as raw geometry (`basis: 'geometry'`), and the preview and inspector say so. Evidence: `test/drawing-import-dimensioning.test.ts` (the foot import, a counterbored-plate import and an L-step import whose inner faces are marked, each placed clear of the rest of the sheet; v49 validation), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts` and `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts`, where the history foot now passes with eight dimensions as well. Slice F: section-aware dimensioning. The checker and Auto-dimension measure a D03 section view's exact cut edges, so a position inside the part (a bore's floor, a pocket's depth) is stated and placed on the visible cut instead of on hidden lines or a rim's hidden silhouette; a requirement only a section marks is reported as "which only section A-A shows". What Auto-dimension can place only on hidden lines is named in its preview ("Add a section through the part to dimension … on visible lines"); it never adds a section itself. A section the kernel cut from the tessellation carries no dimensions and the advice says so. Evidence: `test/drawing-section-dimensioning.test.ts` (real kernel: a Ø20 × 30 bushing with a blind Ø8 bore gets 0–12 and 0–30 on section A-A with zero missing, redundant and unmeasurable, and a 30 − 12 depth is flagged as a closed chain; a pocketed block's floor moves from hidden lines onto the section; the turned foot's advice, and its exact cut refused on the pin) and `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (Auto-dimension places Y on a section). Angles and counterbore callouts. A slope square to one model axis is a requirement, not unmeasurable: one angle per plane (mirrored copies share it) and one corner locating it where it meets a face square to an axis (a real edge before a virtual sharp through a blend); its other corners are derived, and a compound slope stays unmeasurable. The check reads an existing D02 angle dimension between an edge of the slope and an axis-aligned edge in its plane, and Auto-dimension places one in the view along the slope. Schema v50 adds a counterbored hole's callout on a diameter (`counterbore`, the bore's rim): it reads "2× Ø6 THRU, CBORE Ø11 × 3 DEEP" from the model and states the hole and bore diameters and the bore's (and a blind hole's) depth, so Auto-dimension places one callout instead of two diameters and a floor span. A bore's floor must face its opening, so a stepped shaft is never read as one. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (sloped part: 135° angle, its corner, R5), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts`, `test/drawing-import-dimensioning.test.ts` (imported counterbored plate: callout, three requirements stated, a second height span repeats it; v50 validation). Check and placement (PR #1134). Missing lists only what Auto-dimension can place: a position counts as marked only by an edge a dimension can name and only where one view marks it with the datum; otherwise it is reported as not dimensionable yet, with why. A blend tangent to two non-parallel planes is derived (centre and tangent lines). Finding rows are keyed by body and index. Spans ending on an interior feature are placed inside the view through open space; leaders try short placements beside the feature first; text keeps off the editor's view names. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (duplicate findings, shared-hash positions), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts` (sloped blend), `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (hole located inside its view, overall sizes outside); the drawing suites joined the CI smoke selection. Countersink callouts and outside angles (schema v51). A hole size group whose every copy carries a coaxial cone of one chamfer group opening onto it is countersunk; one diameter callout naming the cone's outer rim reads "2× Ø6 THRU, CSK Ø12 × 90°" from the model and states the hole and the cone (instead of a chamfer dimension), and a blind hole's depth. An angle dimension can turn one ray onto its edge's extension (`outside`), so Auto-dimension states a slope as 45° off the part rather than 135° inside. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (Hole-feature countersunk plate: one callout, no chamfer, nothing missing; sloped part: 45° outside), `test/drawing-import-dimensioning.test.ts` (v51 validation). Bolt-circle pitch diameters are placed: Auto-dimension states each unstated bolt circle with the v48 Ø span between two opposite holes' centres, in a view along its axis where the pair lines up across the page ("Ø20" on the 4× Ø3 flange). An odd count has no opposite pair and is reported for a note. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (flange: one Ø span, centre anchors, nothing missing or skipped, stated = required). | D02; D03 for internal features; M05 recognizers for imports. |

## Parallel kernel and web reliability lane

These are consumer requirements. Before a Remus implementation task, refresh its
own ledger and the exact OpenZCAD pin: inherited P-Class numbers and old defect
reports are not proof that the current upstream kernel is missing an operation.
A kernel merge closes a consumer row only after pin adoption and product tests.

| <a id="k01"></a>K01 | Partial | Reproduce remaining tangency/operand-loss, cap-offset, thin-bore tessellation and silent-success examples on the pin; fix or type-refuse upstream, then retire only the workarounds whose replacement passes the original oracles. The exact-only boolean policy now refuses by category instead of shipping a faceted result, and the boolean arm of the face-count census is retired; approximation is measured as no rescue (#330). NURBS-trimmed cylinder display regression: `test/step-nurbs-trimmed-cylinder.test.ts` (synthetic exact STEP, wall chord bound and exact roundtrip); [local private-source evidence](docs/qa/2026-09-19/trimmed-cylinder-render.md) is recorded separately. Concave planar display faces now validate triangle winding and area, then use bounded ear clipping instead of the overlapping vertex fan for valid concave boundaries ([remus#601](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/601)); its synthetic repeated-fin native and installed-WASM regressions ship on the `cb13b53` pin (merged upstream as [remus#606](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/606)). Extrude side walls: the `74422f0` pin reads a nearly straight polynomial bezier as a circle (a 1e-5 fit) and builds a cylinder wall that misses its own bezier cap edges, so every exact boolean that trims the wall refuses (Open Sans `b` at em 10; about 2 % of bundled glyph segments at some size). The adapter splits such curves exactly into pieces the kernel reads as lines (`kernelSafeBezierPieces`; kernel canary and on-face regressions in `test/text-kernel-build.test.ts`) until upstream checks the circle; the same PR certifies away a trimmed B-spline face's untrimmed box (`refineBoundsAtSplineFaces`), which read an engraved 10 mm slab as 10.01. Reduced on the pin, and still present on `c2cd99d0`, to any open cup whose bounding-box centre line passes through a round through-hole. The kernel's internal sub-face classifier calls the material inside such a cup outside, so every boolean with a tool touching it refuses exact-only, and a fuse of a tool wholly inside the wall answers `ok` with the tool's volume added. The adapter stays fail-closed (typed refusals; the union connectivity gate catches the silent `ok`, though its "disconnected groups" message misdescribes overlapping bodies). A generated seam lying on a sphere is checked against that sphere too. When the kernel cuts one face pair's intersection loop into several pieces around a cylinder wall, each piece is named `…between.<pair>.piece.i.of.n` by its midpoint angle about the wall axis, measured from the wall's single seam line. This follows the existing straight-edge piece rule. It fails closed with no single seam, a midpoint within 1e-3 rad of the seam, tied angles, or a piece off the wall. The native edit requires complete coaxial bore rings, retained trims outside both radii, and analytic separation from unrelated surfaces. Cases that change that topology use a cutter spanning the entire body along the bore axis, so its end caps cannot leave shelves or pockets below the chamfer. An enlargement beyond the outer mouth consumes the chamfer cleanly; it does not preserve the mouth's width or move its outer rim. `test/through-hole-chamfer-resize.test.ts` covers shrink/re-enlarge sequences on one and two mouths, native and STEP inputs, before and beyond the outer rim, exact volume, face census, bore length, watertight mesh, immutable input, STEP round-trip, and tilted/mirrored 30/60-degree chamfers. Exact coincidence with the outer rim still refuses on the pinned kernel without changing the source; no diameter or tolerance is nudged to bypass that refusal. `test/e2e/through-hole-chamfer-resize.spec.ts` checks the visible worker preview and applied result through a shrink, outward drag, larger exact entry, undo/redo, and device-storage reload. Face counts and axial length detect the old hidden cutter-cap shelf independently of the diameter label. Stored UV traces and unproved native widening clearances remain outside the local reconstruction family and require a successful exact boolean. Shrinking a countersunk bore may extend its conical mouth when that mouth breaks through a side face. The complete bore ring and strictly valid coaxial cone certify the local edit; outer trims, carriers and unrelated edges stay unchanged. Regressions assert analytic added volume, strict B-Rep validity, unchanged topology/outer curves and immutable source, including the growing holder's two mounting bores. Widening and unsupported UV cases still refuse. The #1258 cone-to-cone shoulder fillet is qualified on paired Remus 2026.1.129 through the production adapter. The regression constructs the reported circle, 23 mm extrusion with -33 degree draft, 6.1 mm upper-rim chamfer and 1 mm shoulder fillet. Its five-face result keeps a closed, consistently wound display mesh through preview, commit, Undo/Redo, fresh JSON history replay and STEP export/reimport. The fit-within-modeling-tolerance advisory remains explicit; the missing-strip mesh advisory must not appear. The case is in the root CI smoke selection (`test/cone-shoulder-fillet.test.ts`). This is headless adapter qualification, not browser or production visual verification. A cylinder/block seam's two concave edges and adjoining convex top edge can be selected in one fillet feature. Regression coverage checks all six selection orders, material changes, one undo/redo step and the browser flow (`mixed-seam-fillet.test.ts`, `mixed-seam-fillet.spec.ts`). Legacy planar-filter counts no longer claim that untested edge subsets work or cannot be rounded. The kernel uses its shared touching-feature cascade. The paired package also enables display-only drag previews; the preview/checked-cache regression and switch-scoping tests run in the routine unit selection with this kernel pin. The kernel's approximation policy remains unchanged; general mixed corners remain unqualified. Later rim fillets retain unchanged curved support boundaries and their authoritative parameter ranges through the paired Remus update. The native rounded-box regression uses an exact rational elliptical crown, three scales, unchanged-input bytes and a closed-form removed-volume oracle. Complete saved-history consumer acceptance covers a 1 mm bottom fillet after existing fillets, fresh replay, Undo/Redo and valid STEP export/reimport (`test/fillet-retained-boundaries.test.ts`). Private input remains outside Git; the test uses `FILLET_REPLAY_PROJECT` and `FILLET_REPLAY_EDGE_LENGTH` in a millimetre document. Existing approximation warnings remain disclosed. This qualifies retained support trims, not general seam-crossing blends. Paired Remus 2026.1.111 enables an exact isolated chamfer through earlier convex cylindrical endpoint rounds, with orthogonal whole-body planar supports and fail-closed neighborhood checks (Remus PR #1517). The 2.1 mm regression checks independent section volume, undo/redo, cold replay, and STEP export; the full-render browser regression selects the edge through the viewport, applies the chamfer, reloads, and validates the downloaded solid. A separate FreeCAD check confirms the exported volume and both cylindrical faces. This qualifies the reproduced geometric pattern, not arbitrary blended bodies. A single refused fillet or chamfer also no longer advises selecting fewer edges. When smaller probes fail and topology cannot name a cause, the message identifies the neighboring faces without claiming every size was ruled out (`edge-modifier-fallback.test.ts`). 2026-11-08 the owner's original case resizes directly on Remus `d370889` (2026.1.126): the R2-filleted boss on the sphere goes from r 6 to 7, 5, 6.3 and 12 as a local direct edit. The adapter now grants `resizeCylindricalFaceDetailed` its `allowApproximate` permission. The kernel removes the seam band, resizes the sharp wall and rolls the measured ball back on in one call. An exact (torus) band still comes back exact or refuses; only the freeform band, which was already a NURBS fit, is rebuilt approximately. The edit carries an advisory that says so (owner decision 2026-11-06). Each result has one wall at the new radius and one band, is closed and STEP-exportable, and matches the volume of the source route (the Cylinder primitive resized, then filleted) to within 0.05 mm³ (`test/sphere-boss-seam-fillet-resize.test.ts`). A radius the ball no longer rolls on (r 1) refuses and keeps the body. 2026-11-05 WS6 ladder on the owner's original case, a direct resize of the boss on the sphere with an R2 seam fillet: measured blocked on Remus `669d1e2`, so it refuses with the typed cause and keeps the body. The edit refuses `direct-edit/unsupported/unsupported_move_face` (`unsupported_neighbor`, `nurbs`) in about 1.1 s for r 6→7 and 6→5. The seam fillet rebuilds in 4.7 s, not minutes. No ladder step exists on this pin: the off-axis cylinder/sphere band has no exact form (`filletDetailed` exact-only gives `exact_only_unattainable`; the default build is one approximate walking NURBS face). `getBlendRegion`, `resizeBlend(…, 0)` and `removeBlendsJournaled` refuse a NURBS band, and `defeature` caps only planar wound neighbours. Even the coaxial boss's exact torus band refuses removal (`unsupported analytic support pair: cylinder x sphere`). Tripwires pin each step, so a pin that adds one fails and names it (`test/sphere-boss-seam-fillet-resize.test.ts`). The K10 matrix excludes sphere×cylinder `fillet-seam`, so no ledger cell changes. Fillet acceptance can certify a loose spline control-hull box using only the affected faces at a finer chord tolerance. The source envelope tolerance, volume-change guard and blend-band guard remain in force; missing bindings or tessellation work limits refuse safely (`fillet-envelope.test.ts`). This fixes an imported-part R1.5 false refusal without replacing its geometry. Full video workflow qualification remains separate. 2026-11-05 WS6 ladder step 2 for a cylindrical wall resize: remove the blends incident to the wall, resize the sharp wall, rebuild the blends (`exact-direct-edit-reblend.ts`). It runs only after the plain resize refuses. Recognition reads every curved neighbour of the wall through the kernel's `getBlendRegion` proof and requires each band to roll between the wall and one exact plane or cylinder support carrier; a chamfer, sphere, freeform or partial band fails closed and the original typed refusal stands. Bands are removed with `resizeBlend(…, 0)`, rebuilt exact-only (`filletDetailed`) and accepted only when the rebuilt bands are proven regions of the recorded classes and radii, the re-blend volume mirrors the removal, and the existing `assertWallResizeAccepted` gates pass. The result is one direct-edit row with no output lineage claim (faces re-resolve by fingerprint). K10 on Remus `2026.1.123`: six cells move from typed refusal to qualified against independent oracles, with no other state change: box×cylinder fillet-seam `direct-grow` (aligned and off-centre, union and subtract) and the imported twin's upright R2 `wall-grow` / `wall-shrink`, so R1–R3 now pass on the upright synthetic twin. Tilted and chamfered-seam R2 cells are unchanged (`exact-direct-edit-reblend.test.ts`, `cylinder-radius-across-boolean.test.ts`). | Kernel C4/C5, P-Class M5/M6; pin update and broader families remain | <a id="k10"></a>K10 | Partial | Combination qualification matrix: generate operand family × placement × boolean × modifier × follow-up edit histories, run them through the production adapter on the installed pin, and keep a committed cell ledger (Qualified, Typed refusal, Untyped refusal, Wrong) with ratcheted CI. Every case must end in exact success (valid closed solid, watertight mesh, independent volume oracle, replay determinism, resolving references, direct/parametric route equivalence) or a typed refusal; untyped errors and the generic sentence fail. Failures shrink to minimal histories that become consumer pins and Remus reproduction bundles. Seeded by the 2026-11-05 boss-on-sphere resize reproduction (untyped `UnsupportedMoveFace` on sphere and blend neighbours). Plan, workstreams and milestones: [kernel robustness plan](docs/plans/kernel-robustness-plan-2026-11-05.md). 2026-11-05 widened combination campaign (WS1). `test/support/combination-matrix.ts` now generates cells from data, with stable ids. It covers seven operand pairs, nine placements and three scales; union, subtract and intersect in both orders; seam and unrelated-edge fillets and chamfers and a cap offset; and ten follow-ups. Each cell is checked against independent oracles (`test/support/combination-oracle.ts`: closed forms, inclusion–exclusion with chord-integrated overlap, Pappus and prism blend sections), against replay determinism (undo/redo, reopen), against route equivalence (direct and source resize, removing a blend and deleting the fillet), and against a STEP round trip. A cell lands in one of five states: qualified, typed refusal, untyped refusal, wrong or timeout. A shrinker (`K10_SHRINK=1`) and a minimal-repro printer cover failing cells. On the `2026.1.122` pin, the 1,392 cells are 725 qualified, 399 typed refusals, 216 untyped refusals and 94 wrong. The CI slice (`test/combination-matrix.test.ts`, 42 cells, `combination-matrix.ledger.json`) stays in the smoke list. The full campaign (`test/combination-matrix.full.test.ts`, 1,350 cells, `combination-matrix.full.ledger.json`) runs in the full suite. Wrong cells: a box minus a half-embedded torus returns the box unchanged; a direct wall resize under a seam blend loses the blend volume; an internally tangent cross drill is 2.2 mm³ high; and the Steinmetz cross drill is 0.071 mm³ off. Phase 3 local direct-edit qualification on the supplied native/STEP boss/block fixtures with the paired Remus 2026.1.125 PR1582 overlay. The [before/after report](docs/qa/video-parity-phase3.md) separates kernel improvements, app wiring, cumulative limitations, exact refusals and measured latency. App changes expose analytic native blend edits, visible radius validation, single-pick bottom-chain chamfer and the typed diameter refusal. Dependency pins remain unchanged; the kernel-dependent paths need requalification after the paired Remus refresh. No production or complete-video parity claim is made. Round 2 qualifies the combined hash-identified packages, adds prepared blend/boss drag frames and serialized cancellation, and routes shouldered cap shortening through the quality-aware local move. The [round-2 report](docs/qa/video-parity-phase3-round2.md) records real pointer-frame latency, the corrected R1-rim fixture and A3 pick, explicit A4 tangent selection, and separate full-order versus finishing-chain outcomes. Sampled cap geometry is disclosed in Diagnostics; no sampled result is labelled exact. Round 3 adds opt-in Chrome/worker profiling, disposable prepared-drag display quality, and cache keys that preserve actual boolean probe decisions while removing equivalent selection/idle replays. The [performance report](docs/qa/video-parity-phase3-round3.md) records faster warm frames, exact canonical/cancel/Undo preservation, the release-quality tradeoff, and remaining measured kernel costs. Neither latency target is claimed as qualified; dependency pins remain unchanged and PR #1276 stays on hold. Round 4 refreshes both immutable dependency pins to Remus main a4ca5422 and verifies the actual browser wasm hashes. The [round-4 report](docs/qa/video-parity-phase3-round4.md) records the strict R1.5 one-body video order separately from the kernel's R1.9 W14 control, including the newly accepted concave R2 edit and preserved bodies after refusals. The app defers unrelated planar capability proofs, caches immutable preview-source measurements, and warms full-quality display meshes once per stable gesture. Canonical acceptance, full display quality and geometry remain authoritative. The PR remains on hold; this is local qualification, not production evidence. The CI follow-up preserves ordinary single-edge Chamfer and offers a separate named tangent-chain action from a qualified bottom pick. The existing smoke journey retains its volume/export/reload assertions; native and STEP seven-edge A6 selection/chamfer/Undo reruns pass. The round-4 report records the regression and the updated UI route without replacing earlier timing evidence. 2026-11-05 imported-twin R-slice (WS1, reference scenarios R1–R9). `test/support/imported-twin.ts` builds a synthetic twin of the recorded body: block, r 6 post, seam, rim and corner blends. It exports the twin to STEP and edits the `importStepBody` import, which has no construction history. Oracles are closed forms, Pappus sections and the integrated overlap; one tangent-ended edge uses a labelled interval. On Remus `2026.1.123` the 74 cells are 28 qualified, 1 typed refusal, 43 untyped refusals and 2 wrong; with #1265 and #1266 merged, the four R2 cells refuse typed and no cell is wrong. The CI slice holds 5 cells (`combination-matrix.imported-twin.ledger.json`); the full campaign holds 69 (`combination-matrix.imported-twin.full.ledger.json`). The tilted post's seam takes neither a fillet nor a chamfer, so the edits run on upright and Y-tilted unblended fallback twins. R1, R3, R4 and R9 qualify. R2 is wrong on the upright twin: the wall resizes and both blend bands stay on their r 6 carriers. On the tilted twin R2 refuses untyped. R5 and R6 qualify at r 1.5 and with an equal-radius r 4 chain, but refuse at r 3. R7's refusals keep the body and use plain sentences, but none is typed and negative sizes are refused only during the rebuild. Local reference-workflow baseline on the supplied boss/block native and STEP fixtures with a process-local paired Remus 2026.1.124 overlay, without feature or kernel changes. [Scope, gaps and reproduction](docs/qa/video-parity-baseline.md) and [per-attempt measurements](docs/qa/video-parity-measurements.md) distinguish isolated success, approximation, UI/lineage gates and exact kernel refusal. The actual browser wasm response hashes match the committed package bytes. This is local evidence, not production or complete cumulative-video qualification. PR #1279 qualifies the a4ca5422 paired Remus refresh against sphere-boss refusal and axial-history regressions. Exact-only approximate-reblend refusals retain their typed cause, show clear product copy, and stop pointless waypoint retries. Exported reproductions retain that same classification and sentence. The newly supported coaxial torus-band removal is asserted closed and valid; the remaining off-axis freeform WS6 limitations stay explicit. Axial equivalence cases retain every geometry, reference and cache assertion with cold-replay timeouts and opt-in cold-versus-cached timing evidence. Kernel size limits and load-time review requirements are unchanged. This does not implement WS6 reblend or claim production qualification. Union connectivity no longer calls overlapping box×sphere bodies disconnected. The pin has no certified box/sphere distance and refuses their exact intersection, so an overlap that was never proven used to be reported as "2 disconnected groups". `sharedInteriorPoint` (`exact-boolean-helpers.ts`) now proves shared volume from a point that both `classifyPoint` and `classifyPointRobust` place strictly inside both solids, probed only in the operands' common box. Tangent, stacked, gapped and near-corner pairs yield no witness and still refuse as disconnected. The 27 box×sphere raised and sliver union cells keep `untyped-refusal`, but now with the correct reason: the kernel's fuse fails strict validation ("1 shared edges have inconsistent face orientations"), which is an upstream defect, not a connectivity fault. Analytic witness checks, closed-form cap volumes, and adapter regressions on both sides are in `test/union-connectivity.test.ts`. This also answers the misdescription noted under K01 (QA CAD-001). 2026-11-05 acceptance gates for the K10 `wrong` cells (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/result-acceptance.ts`, `result-acceptance.test.ts`). A subtract whose overlap could not be measured refuses as `cut_kept_tool_material` when a point the kernel classifies inside the target, the tool and the result proves the cut kept material. A direct wall resize refuses as `resize_left_blend_behind` when a curved face meeting the wall no longer meets the resized wall, and as `resize_volume_mismatch` when its volume change is beyond 3× of the swept sleeve. Cuts and intersects are held to strict validation. On the `669d1e2` pin, 81 wrong cells become typed refusals (box − torus 70, seam-blend resize 8, tangent drill 3), plus one grazing drill whose B-rep fails strict validation. The ledgers stand at 724 qualified, 521 typed, 176 untyped and 13 wrong (including #1265's typed resize refusals). The 13 Steinmetz cells stay wrong: their body is exact (exact ellipse edges, clean validation), and the kernel's volume integration is 1.25e-5 low, which is reported upstream. | K03 infrastructure; feeds K01, K05, K07, K08, W04 and A01 priorities. Launcher font-parser split (2026-10-14 project review PERF-3): opentype.js left the preloaded `geometry` chunk (296,291 → 58,131 B raw; start-screen transfer 558,128 → 490,776 B and FCP 990 → 928 ms at 4× CPU, 9 Mbps/150 ms RTT), and `report-bundle-sizes --check` now fails when a launcher chunk carries it (`test/bundle-size-policy.test.ts`). 14 October review follow-up PERF-4: the entry chunk is within a few hundred bytes of its budget and App.tsx is about half of it, because the start screen loads the whole workspace; split a thin launcher and preload the workspace on idle or hover ([project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)). The device-only library paint shown before account discovery now uses the same pinned-then-custom-order comparator as the merged listing, so reloading no longer draws cards in storage key order and reshuffles them when the account listing lands. Projects only the account holds still appear when that listing arrives. Coverage: `localProjectStore.test.ts` (saved shelf order, not key order). Library startup publishes existing device summaries while account discovery continues, then returns the cloud listing before encrypted card metadata has finished. Names and thumbnail references still pass the existing signature, epoch, authority and rollback checks, with four verifications in flight and session-scoped, batched presentation updates. Health, authentication config, settings, collaboration config and speculative chunk prefetch no longer block the initial library. Selecting a project immediately shows its named loading screen with cancellation; document reconciliation still precedes hydration, and the file count loads afterward. Regression coverage includes 150 encrypted cards, delayed optional services, cached rows, rejected/stale metadata, local settings changed during discovery, shared viewers awaiting capabilities, cancellation during device writes, superseded navigation and late configuration responses after retries or cloud-mode changes (`progressive-loading.spec.ts`, `apiProjectListing.test.ts`, `progressiveShelf.test.ts`, `projectShelf.test.ts`, `cloudConfigurationReads.test.ts`). Original-account production timing and target-hardware median/p95 qualification remain open. Library refresh preserves cached project names and card order while encrypted metadata is unverified, without changing transport versions, storage formats, organization precedence or late-verification conflict fences. Thumbnails keep their decoded image during refresh and on returning to the library, switch only after the replacement decodes, and retain visibility-based loading. The bounded memory cache scopes protected previews to the account and key session; account changes, key teardown and unsupported formats cannot reuse protected images or accept late results. Regression coverage exercises provisional listings, verified renames, decode failures, empty projects, stale requests, remounts and session invalidation (`PartThumbnail.test.tsx`, `apiProjectListing.test.ts`, `projectShelf.test.ts`, and the smoke-tagged thumbnail continuity journey in `progressive-loading.spec.ts`). Account erasure, sign-out with or without a stored-device choice, and cross-tab key teardown also remove title hints (`accountErasure.test.ts`, `signOut.test.ts`, `encryption-session.spec.ts`). Target-hardware median/p95 qualification remains open. The repair integrates current main without dropping either side's unit manifest entries. Static smoke assertions and weight refreshes now exclude the changed-file overlay, with real Git-fixture coverage for untagged additions, edits and deletions. Settings search includes the touch-history and Pencil controls. The workspace-switch Revolve regression waits for a ready non-text keyboard target; its original failure reproduced twice under concurrency, then all 12 repeated spec executions passed with two workers and no retries. Full root validation also exposed an intersection symbol introduced on main without font coverage. The pinned math-font subset and its CSS range now include U+2229, with an explicit glyph regression assertion and a passing interface-wide coverage audit. Further complete browser qualification exposed stale harness assumptions: the rail test now resolves its CSS length through layout, the contrast test opens the keypad before measuring its unit buttons, and camera regressions start gestures on measured bare canvas rather than covered fixed coordinates. The checkpoint-module fault test uses Save-Data to isolate the explicit import from idle prefetch, retaining its exact request-count and persistence checks. The imperial-unit refusal reads the validation warning, and the unresolved extrude waits for History's failed-feature state before opening its editor. The datum repair journey re-projects the known face after workspace changes; its old pixel was proven to hit empty canvas. The gear preview journey waits for committed undo/redo heights before reload, and encrypted STEP coverage requires exactly one import source apart from separately encrypted thumbnails, retaining the all-object ciphertext and fresh-browser checks. Extrude repair also waits for the canceled editor to unmount and projects the roof-face center directly from History's action menu. Constraint-control hit tests re-scroll after flyout reflow. Gear previews retain all six asserted reversal targets and committed drag paths while omitting unobserved pointer interpolations, preserving every assertion and the 120-second deadline. Evidence validation reuses parsed row IDs within a fold instead of parsing the full wide ledger per fragment. A 250-row regression retains escaped/code pipes and validates every resulting row; all refusal rules and the five-second deadline remain unchanged. The ASCII STL tokenizer now scans native token matches with the same six ASCII separators, preserving lookahead, grammar and refusal behavior. Eight separator regressions and the existing 500,000/500,001-triangle boundary tests pass under coverage without extending their five-second deadlines. The four formerly failing journeys also pass in isolation; complete hosted qualification remains the queue-admission requirement. Full browser qualification uses one attempt and retains failure contexts; routine PR browsers run critical and changed journeys, with complete units on broad inputs. The exact 10,000-object 3MF acceptance and 10,001-object refusal are independent parser cases, each using the existing test deadline. All limit values, rejection responses, numeric geometry, export, undo and persistence assertions remain. ## Later product reach and explicit deferrals

| ID | Status | Outcome / prerequisite | Legacy scope |

| <a id="l02"></a>L02 | Partial | iPad, touch and Apple Pencil, selected 2026-11-05 ([plan](docs/plans/ipad-touch-plan-2026-11-05.md)): iPadOS Safari scaling and safe areas, touch navigation and selection in the 3D viewport, replacements for hover and keyboard, touch sketching, a Pencil mode (Pencil draws and selects, the finger navigates), an installable Home Screen app with persistent storage, and rendering budgets measured on iPad. Choose behaviour by `pointerType`, so mouse and trackpad input on any device stays unchanged. Eight bounded slices; device-only behaviour needs real-iPad evidence before a slice is Done. Preserve offline behavior and accessible input. Slice 6, Apple Pencil. - After a Pencil is used, a finger in a sketch drawing tool only navigates. This is controlled by Settings › Viewport "Only Apple Pencil draws" (default on), a new app-settings field that the Worker carries. - Palm rejection: fingers are ignored while a pen is down and for 300 ms after it lifts, and a landing pen cancels a finger gesture in progress. - The Pencil keeps the mouse path: precise taps, drags, marquee and hover. Evidence: - `touchNavigation.test.ts` (navigation-only fingers, abandoning a palm) - `app-settings.test.ts` - `api-routes.test.ts` (the setting is carried across an older client's save) - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, driving a real pen and fingers through Chromium's input domain. Mutation-checked: with Pencil mode forced off, the finger draws. Not yet verified on an iPad: Pencil hover and Safari's own palm rejection. Slice 7, the Home Screen app and files. - **Installable:** a web manifest and Apple Home Screen tags. - **Installed-app only:** persistent storage and an offline shell (`sw.js`). The shell is network-first for pages and cache-first for content-hashed assets. It is never registered by a browser tab, the desktop shell or a test build. - **Exports** open the share sheet on a touch screen that can share files, and otherwise download. - **Import pickers** drop the `accept` filter on iPad and iPhone, so `.step`, `.openzcad` and `.shapr` files can be chosen. - **Download fix:** blob URLs are no longer revoked at click time (WebKit). Evidence: - `saveFile.test.ts`, including the revoke regression - `desktopBridge.test.ts` - `appleTouch.test.ts`, covering the manifest, pickers and storage - `offlineShell.test.ts`, which runs the shipped `sw.js` against an in-memory cache and a scripted network Not yet verified on an iPad: installing to the Home Screen, launching offline, and the share sheet. Slice 1, screen scaling and iOS basics: - The shell and stage size to `100dvh`, so Safari's toolbars no longer cut off the bottom of the workspace. - Touch pointers get 44px rail, command and Done/Cancel targets, and 16px fields so iOS does not zoom the page into a focused value. - Over the model, a press-and-hold no longer selects text or shows a callout, and the page no longer rubber-bands. - The numeric keypad no longer raises the system keyboard over its own keys. A touch on the field itself raises it, and the keypad stays above it. Evidence: - `touchContracts.test.ts` and `NumericKeypad.test.tsx`. - `test/e2e/tablet-layout.spec.ts`: six iPad sizes under Chromium touch emulation. Real-iPad verification is still pending. Follow-up to [#1230](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/1230): keep a long press's release click suppressed for the entire hold, then start the one-second delayed-click grace period when its pointer ends. A new deliberate press clears leftover suppression after a cancelled hold or a missing click. The focused `touchLongPress.test.ts` suite passes all eight cases using the repository's Vitest 5.0.5 and happy-dom 21.0.4 versions in an isolated local runner. Regressions cover holding the menu open for five seconds, a new tap after cancellation, and expiry after release. Restoring the original module makes the long-hold and cancellation regressions fail. This is local regression evidence; the updated PR heads still require the full CI gates. Slice 8, rendering limits. Desktop rendering is unchanged. - **Smaller shadow map:** touch devices render the key-light shadow at 2048² instead of 3072². - **Lower navigation resolution on slow devices:** a device under 45 fps navigates at a 1.25 pixel ratio and returns to its full ratio at rest. - **Context-loss recovery:** a lost WebGL context comes back drawing, with its environment and shadow map rebuilt. Evidence: - `renderBudget.test.ts` - `shadowMapSize.test.ts` - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, where a real `WEBGL_lose_context` loss and restore leaves the viewer drawing with its shadows rebuilt iPad memory and frame timing remain device measurements under W01. Slice 2, touch gesture recognizer: `packages/viewport/src/input/touchGestures.ts` turns touch pointer samples into gestures: - tap and double tap - long press - one-finger drag - two-finger pinch (midpoint pan and spread scale) - two- and three-finger taps The timing rules are covered too: - a second finger takes over from the first; - the finger left behind after a pinch does nothing; - a press the host claims for a handle yields nothing more; - `pointercancel` cancels what was in progress. It is not yet wired to the viewport; that is slice 3. Evidence: `touchGestures.test.ts`, which is also in the unit smoke selection. Slice 4, stand-ins for hover, keys and right-click. - **Viewer-bar toggles**, shown to a coarse pointer only: - Multi-select: taps add or remove, as Shift or Ctrl does. - Free values: no snaps or inference, as Shift does. - **Long press**: a finger held 550 ms anywhere in the chrome opens that element's context menu (History rows, Assemble parts and joints), or else shows its tooltip. The click from the lift is swallowed. - **Settings › Viewport** gains "Undo with two fingers" (default on). It is a new app-settings field: the Worker accepts it as optional and keeps the stored value when an older client saves without it. Evidence: - `touchLongPress.test.ts` - `Tooltip.test.tsx` - `ViewerToolbar.test.tsx` - `app-settings.test.ts` - `api-routes.test.ts`, with a mutation check that the stored value is carried - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, covering the Multi-select toggle and a held History row Slice 5, touch sketching. - A finger gets twice the screen tolerance for: - sketch, move, offset and measure snaps; - the sketch grab dot and the rotate ring's band; - curve picks. - While a finger drags sketch geometry, a magnifier above it shows the hidden point at 2× with a crosshair and names the snap it took. Evidence: - `pointerSlack.test.ts` - `session.test.ts` (finger grab and ring band) - `touchLoupe.test.ts` - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`: a finger draws a circle, and the loupe shows and then clears. Slice 3, touch on the 3D viewport. On empty space: - one finger orbits (it pans in a sketch); - two fingers pan and pinch-zoom about their midpoint; - a tap selects; - a double tap acts as a double click; - a held finger opens the pick list or context menu; - two- and three-finger taps undo and redo. A first finger that lands on a handle, gizmo, footprint, armed face or sketch-tool stroke keeps the mouse behaviour, and other fingers cannot disturb it. Touch picks edges in a band about 15 px wide. Safari's page pinch is suppressed over the viewport. Evidence: - `touchNavigation.test.ts` - `CameraController.test.ts` (touch pinch) - `PickService.test.ts` (touch edge band) - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, real multi-touch through Chromium's input domain The existing touch and mouse viewport specs are unchanged. Device timing is not yet verified on an iPad. | Product P-1/P-2. |

Open: `sketch-mode.css` orphans, the unused `ExtrudePreview` type in `ModelViewer.tsx`, and two unreferenced exports that need a public-API decision. 14 October review follow-up TEST-7: establish a flaky-test budget for the E2E gate; failures still block delivery ([project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)). 14 October review follow-up TEST-8: `@openzcad/geometry` in io-step, `@openzcad/io-step` in kernel-adapter and the root `@typescript-eslint/{eslint-plugin,parser}` are unused, and `.github/workflows/trusted-vps.yml` is an active echo stub (stub removed in the 19 October CI review) ([project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)). 2026-10-21 CI reliability: Playwright now caps CI at two browser workers instead of scaling from the physical host CPU count; the configuration regression checks effective concurrency and preserves test deadlines and retries. With the explicit readiness assertions, 20 no-retry CI-configured repetitions and all three copy browser cases passed locally. The four Apply/Cancel transition checks and all copy-state and body-count assertions remain intact, with unchanged deadlines and retry configuration. Delayed fetches retain the same Python implementation, and the dispatcher retains the missing immutable object guard before preparation. Offline, stale-pin, main-ancestry, exact-fetch count and real Vitest control/prepared assertions remain intact. A serial local comparison reduced the cold regression from 3.31 to 2.71 seconds and the nested deadline experiment from 7.83 to 7.53 seconds; those measurements do not guarantee Hosted timing. The five-second test, ten-second setup, nine-second child-process limit and six-second delayed-fetch control are unchanged, as are active workflow pins and runtime routing. Focused verification passes all 34 policy/history tests. Required local lint, type checking, root tests (7,312 passed, eight skipped), web tests (3,828 passed), parity corpus and build pass. The Python 3.9 fixture and the direct/queue policy suites also pass. Real shallow Git-history fixtures complement the fleet setup deadline regression: cold and partly warm checkouts retain a distinct candidate HEAD, prepare both immutable snapshots through a single bounded main-history transfer, and work offline once prepared. Missing comparison data and fetched off-main active or comparison pins fail closed. Comparison provenance uses the same immutable pin definition as the queue-policy oracle. Worktree edits cannot replace pinned bytes, including a policy larger than 128 KiB. Active workflows, pins, routing assertions and existing test/setup/job deadlines are unchanged. The direct preflight entrypoint also prepares both absent snapshots and their main ancestry in one transfer before Vitest setup, with a real cold-checkout regression. The main-history cutoff precedes both reviewed fixture pins and is an acquisition hint only. Neither a cached snapshot nor the cutoff authorizes a pin: complete immutable reads and actual main-ancestry checks still determine provenance. Cold setup excludes an unrelated older asset, without filtering or truncating workflow blobs. A synthetic candidate HEAD may stay shallow after main is ready; prepared setup remains offline in that case. These cases cover the cold standalone preflight and the unchanged ten-second setup hook without downloading all earlier repository history. UTC `Z` timestamps remain compatible with Python 3.9. 2026-10-22 CI reliability: floating-panel browser tests poll for non-null rendered bounds within the existing assertion budget. A handle re-arm can hide its value-chip HUD for one frame immediately after a visibility assertion; the measurement now waits for that rendered state without changing test timeouts, retries, geometry, overlap or pointer-reachability assertions. The unchanged live-handle case failed 4 of 10 local attempts before the fix; it passed 20 of 20 afterward with retries disabled, and all eight floating-panel cases passed. This repairs the readiness race without closing the separate flaky-test-budget work. CI routing tests prepare immutable Git objects and main history in one main fetch for a cold checkout in suite setup, then retain every routing and provenance assertion under the existing five-second test deadline. An automated six-second fixture fetch reproduces the old timeout; the prepared case passes with unchanged test, setup and job deadlines. The no-network regression proves cold evidence fails closed, all routing checks pass without fetching after preparation with a shallow PR merge HEAD (including when main equals the pin), both initially missing workflow objects become readable after the same fetch, and a stale main ref still rejects the pin (`test/trusted-pr-policy.test.ts`, `scripts/test-fleet-test-fixtures.py`). Active workflow bytes, pins, runner routing and required checks are unchanged. Full local repository verification and the protected PR/queue runs remain delivery gates. The existing fleet-policy provenance reader normalizes Git's UTC `Z` suffix to `+00:00` before parsing its shallow-history date. The cold-fixture regression exposed Python 3.9 rejecting that timestamp before the fetch refusal could run. The unchanged offline, stale-pin and real six-second deadline assertions pass with Python 3.9 and 3.12. Explicit timezone offsets, ancestor checks, immutable workflow pins and test deadlines retain their existing behavior. | Camera 2.3 and E6; absence of old symbols may already close the proposed cleanup. |

## Completion and maintenance rules

1. Refresh main, the pinned kernel and open PR ownership before selecting a
   master ID. Use a new isolated branch; one bounded slice and one ready PR.
   A stacked PR names its dependency. Do not silently switch items when blocked:
   record the missing evidence/input against the selected row.
2. Before coding, record the slice, observable acceptance cases, dependencies
   and any schema/API/rollout decision. Preserve exactness/provenance, units,
   verified topology references, offline persistence and one-transaction undo.
   AI and manual edits use the same validated command path. Never weaken a
   refusal, oracle or tolerance to mark progress.
3. Run the root [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) verification gates and the row's browser,
   geometry or performance checks. Exact-head hosted checks are required before
   a merge decision; queued/skipped jobs are not passing. Deployment and manual
   Apple Silicon execution retain their separate authorization requirements.
4. Do not edit a row in the implementation PR. Add one
   [evidence fragment](docs/roadmap-evidence/README.md)
   (`docs/roadmap-evidence/<row>--<slug>.md`) with the shipped subset,
   evidence (tests plus PR), remaining acceptance and changed dependencies,
   and a `state` only when the status changes. A fragment merges with its
   code, so its state is never "In review": use `Partial` or `Complete`.
   A standalone `docs(roadmap): fold evidence` PR folds fragments into rows.
   Edit this file directly only to add, split or restructure rows. Add a
   linked child ID for a split, never a second untracked plan. Keep detailed
   acceptance specifications linked; do not copy their status tables back here
   as multiple competing versions.
5. Close only the scope proved. Move completed rows into the delivered baseline
   with their stable IDs, or retain a short `Complete` row so links survive.
   A row states the outcome and the remaining work; when its evidence outgrows
   a few sentences, move the history verbatim into
   `docs/roadmap-history/<id>.md` (or its plan or ADR) in a standalone docs PR
   and keep the outcome, a delivered summary, the remaining work and a link in
   the row.
   Record live acceptance separately from merged source. Revalidate old kernel
   claims upstream and old UI findings on the rendered current app before work.
